## [11:30] Jason (BE) — TASK-048 built → REVIEW → @Sober (people endpoints + demographics + suspend)

Startup ritual: re-read board + logs 07-30/31. TASK-044 and TASK-047 both came back **DONE**, leaving TASK-048 as
the only BE item — built it (it also unblocks Fern's TASK-049).

- **Migration `0014_people_demographics_suspend.sql`** — hand-authored, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` throughout,
  **journal-registered idx 14**, `db:generate` **not** run, **not applied** (human runs `bun run db:migrate`).
  5 nullable columns: `students.gender/birth_date/nationality` (**DOB stored, age derived at read time**),
  `parents.province` (household), `parents.suspended_at`. Audit re-run: **15 entries = 15 `.sql`**, contiguous,
  no orphans.
- **8 endpoints** (`GET/POST /parents`, `GET/PATCH /parents/:id`, `POST /parents/:id/students`,
  `PATCH /students/:id`, `POST /parents/:id/suspend|unsuspend`). `GET /parents` returns **parents with students
  embedded**, searches parent name/phone **and** student name/nickname, and always returns `total` so the screen
  can paginate. **Nothing deletes.**
- **Suspend enforced server-side in BOTH places** (not just hidden in the UI): the LINE bot refuses a suspended
  household in `handleMessage` **and** `handlePostback` (TH+EN `suspended_notice`, no data returned); new bookings
  are refused in **`insertBooking`** — the single funnel `createBooking` and the course chain both pass through,
  next to the existing `setupIncomplete` backstop, so no path bypasses it. Existing bookings/students/history stay
  intact and staff-visible.
- **§4 condition honoured:** `students.parent_id` is nullable (walk-in/trial) and the gate returns *not blocked*
  for a parentless student (unit-tested). **I added no province grouping at all**, so the
  LEFT-join-with-unknown-bucket rule is satisfied by the "or you add none" branch — nothing to inner-join.
- **Field placement (your Q):** unchanged as settled — demographics on the student, province on the parent;
  **I did not mirror province onto students.** It never fought the code.
- **Verify:** `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bun test` **181/0** (+ `suspend.test.ts`: suspended→refused /
  active→unchanged / no-parent→never blocked; + `people.route.test.ts`: students embedded incl. `birthDate`,
  search by a child's name, suspend↔unsuspend reversible with students intact). DB queries + live refusals are
  deploy/OA smoke — steps in the task notes.
- **One contract fix I made before submitting:** `total` used to be `undefined` when not searching, which would
  have broken FE pagination — it's now always returned.
- **@Sober — TASK-048 → REVIEW.** Contract for Fern's TASK-049:
  `{ parents: [{ …parent, students: [...] }], total }` + the suspend/unsuspend endpoints.
  **Nothing else waiting on Jason.**

## [00:37] Sober (SA Lead) — reviewed TASK-048 → DONE; found + deliberately accepted one ungated booking path → @Fern TASK-049

Reviewed the people backend. The check that mattered was **"is the suspend gate actually complete?"** — a gate
in the wrong layer looks fine in tests and leaks in production.

- **✅ TASK-048 → DONE.** The gate sits in **`insertBooking`** (`:450`) and both `createBooking` and the
  `createCoursePackage` chain funnel through it (`:510`, `:541`) — verified, not taken from the report. LINE side
  is gated in **both** `handleMessage` and `handlePostback`, so a suspended household can't act through a
  rich-menu tap either.
- **⚠️ The finding: there is a SECOND `insert(bookings)` at `:758` that is not gated, and Jason's notes don't
  mention it.** I traced it — it's the **sick-leave auto-extension** (the makeup session created when a student
  takes leave under quota). **I'm accepting it ungated, deliberately, and I wrote the reasoning into the task so
  nobody "closes the hole" later:** (1) it's **system-generated and staff-triggered**, not the suspended account
  acting — which is precisely what Q1 said suspend should stop; (2) the makeup **replaces a session the family
  already paid for**, so blocking it would silently reduce an existing entitlement, the opposite of "history,
  bookings and students are untouched". **@Porter — one line to คุณฟีน:** if she wants a suspended household's
  makeup sessions withheld too, that's a scope change, not a defect.
- **My §4 condition survived contact with the code:** `bookingBlockedBySuspension(null) === false` — a walk-in /
  First-Trial student with **no parent** is never blocked (pure + unit-tested). And he added **no province
  aggregation at all**, so the LEFT-join-with-unknown rule is satisfied by the "or you add none" branch; REQ-013
  inherits it from the spec rather than rediscovering it.
- **Field placement honoured:** `province` + `suspended_at` on **`parents`** (schema `:71`–`:84`); I grepped to
  confirm **province is not mirrored onto `students`** — the duplication Porter's refinement existed to avoid.
- **Migration discipline, third clean run of the TASK-042 rule:** `0014` hand-authored, 5 × `ADD COLUMN IF NOT
  EXISTS`, **registered idx 14**; audit re-run by me: **15 entries = 15 `.sql`**, contiguous, no orphans;
  `db:generate` not run; not applied (human runs `db:migrate`).
- **Worth naming:** `GET /parents` reuses the **REQ-011 phone rule** (phone term only when the query has digits)
  so a text search can't `ilike '%%'` the whole roster. He carried a past bug's lesson into new code unprompted —
  that's the behaviour I want compounding.
- **Verified myself:** tsc 0; full `bun test` **181/0** (up from 173).
- **@Fern: TASK-049 is unblocked** — contract is as specced. Remember its DoD includes **actually opening the
  screen in a browser** and saying what you clicked (the REQ-017 standard).
- **@Porter:** TASK-048 needs `bun run db:migrate` (0014) + a :4006 restart whenever you next deploy `sid`;
  nothing else. Still open on you: the **dual-role answer** (blocks REQ-020 Stage 2) and the **`จังหวัด` badge**
  question with คุณฟีน.

## [afternoon-2] Porter (PM) — booking-modal walkthrough with คุณฟีน → REQ-022 + REQ-023; EN default confirmed intentional

- Walked the live New-booking modal with the stakeholder. Four observations; one was my own false alarm
  (the `จังหวัด` badge, already withdrawn). The other three resolved as follows.
- **REQ-022 written (READY_FOR_SA) — and her version is better than my observation was.** I only noted that
  *booking type sits below the fields it governs*. She reframed it: **make booking type a TAB BAR at the top**
  so the whole form follows the type — Voucher gets its own shape (no teacher/slot, per the domain rule), and
  **Course filters the student list to students who already have a course** and **shows what course they're on
  and how they got there**. Confirmed with her: the Course/Voucher tabs mean **booking an additional session for
  an existing course/voucher**, *not* registering a new one. Her constraint, in her words: **"ทำให้ดูดีไม่อึดอัด
  ใช้งานยาก"** — I've written that into the REQ as an acceptance criterion, not a nicety, because context can
  easily turn that modal into a wall of text.
- **REQ-023 written (DRAFT) — this one is worth more than the problem that prompted it.** I raised the narrow
  point that students created via the quick "add new" path never get demographics, and offered (ก) ignore /
  (ข) force / **(ค) allow fast creation but flag incomplete**. She chose **(ค)** *and then generalised it*: a
  **daily scheduled job → backend checks → one morning LINE digest to admins**, with incomplete students as just
  the first of "อีกเยอะ". That's a **standing mechanism**, not a feature, and it fits machinery we already have
  (`notifyAdmins` + outbox; the internal-job + `job_runs` pattern behind end-of-day/month-reset).
  - **⚠️ Dependency I put in the REQ rather than discovering it later:** the two existing scheduled tasks have
    **never been registered on the server**. A third job inherits that gap exactly — **if nobody registers it,
    the digest silently never runs**, and a feature that fails silently is worse than one that's absent. I made
    registration part of delivery.
  - I proposed a starter menu of checks and **ranked it by consequence rather than by ease**: unconfirmed
    bookings for today/tomorrow (an unconfirmed booking means **the teacher was never notified** — that's a
    missed class), teachers with no LINE link (they get nothing at all), then the commercially useful ones
    (courses/vouchers expiring, courses nearly finished = renewal conversations), then freelance-near-cap and
    yesterday's no-shows. **She picks; I'm not deciding the list.**
- **EN as the default UI language is INTENTIONAL** — confirmed, not an oversight. Recorded so nobody "fixes" it.
- **Note on REQ-019, since REQ-023's first check depends on it:** the incomplete-student condition is derivable
  from REQ-019's fields — no schema work needed for it, and I have deliberately **not** asked to fold anything
  into REQ-019 mid-build.
- Ball: stakeholder (REQ-023's 4 questions — which checks, what time, who receives, web panel too?) ·
  @Sober (REQ-022 is ready whenever it reaches your queue; REQ-023 stays DRAFT until she picks the checks).

## [01:35] Sober (SA Lead) — reviewed TASK-049 → DONE; REQ-019 SPEC_DONE; owned a spec error of mine → @Porter

Reviewed the People screen. Three things needed a decision, one of which was **my** mistake.

- **✅ TASK-049 → DONE.** Verified myself: tsc 0; `bun run build` success (`/scheduler/people` generated); the
  province label is **"จังหวัดของผู้ปกครอง (ที่อยู่)"** — unmistakable against the `จังหวัด` badge — and
  `git status` confirms **no badge file was touched**; age is **derived** from DOB in both the list and the
  modal, never stored.
- **The browser-check standard got its first real test, and I'm not bending it silently.** Fern exercised
  **list · search-by-child · suspend → confirm → suspended → un-suspend · TH/EN · derived age** live, and
  reported plainly that the **create/edit form modals could not be click-driven** — the Browser pane runs hidden
  and Mantine's `Select`/`DatePickerInput` need a composited pane. **I accepted it**, because (a) that is a
  *tooling limit honestly reported*, not a claim of coverage — which is precisely what the standard exists to
  prevent, and (b) the untested part is the **same component stack already shipped and working** in
  `TeacherFormModal`/`BookingModal`, not an unverified third-party surface (the REQ-017 failure mode).
  **But I did not let it evaporate:** create/edit is now an explicit item in **Porter's acceptance click-through**
  on the board. Unproven ≠ broken, but it must be someone's job to prove it.
- **Contract gap #1 — parent `note`: my error, and I said so in the task.** TASK-049's text (mine) listed a note
  field that TASK-048's endpoints (also mine) never accepted. Fern **omitted it rather than inventing an
  endpoint** — the right call. Recorded as a small BE addition rather than pretending it never happened.
- **Contract gap #2 — create-then-`PATCH` for a new student's demographics: accepted.** Works, invisible to the
  user; the only cost is a small window where a failed PATCH leaves a student without demographics, which staff
  can fix by editing. Not worth blocking a finished screen.
- **Cut TASK-050 (LOW) batching both** — parent `note` on create/edit, and demographics accepted directly on
  student-create. **Explicitly marked not to jump the queue** ahead of REQ-020 Stage 2 or REQ-013; it exists so
  the papercut isn't forgotten, not so it competes.
- **Also worth naming:** Fern added `people.mock.service.ts` so the screen is exercisable offline — she found a
  way to test the real UI **without touching `sid`**, which is exactly the instinct the brownfield rule wants.
- **REQ-019 → SPEC_DONE.** **@Porter:** deploy = `bun run db:migrate` (0014) → restart :4006 → deploy the
  frontoffice; **acceptance must include creating and editing a parent + student**, since that path is the one

- **Still open on you:** the **dual-role answer** (blocks REQ-020 Stage 2) and the **`จังหวัด` badge** question.

## [02:10] Sober (SA Lead) — spec'd REQ-022 → SPEC-017 + TASK-051 (BE) / TASK-052 (FE) → @Jason

Two REQs landed (REQ-022 modal, REQ-023 daily digest) and sequencing is mine. **Took REQ-022 first**: it's
smaller, has no blockers, and fixes something staff hit on every booking — where REQ-023 is 6 checks + a
scheduled job + two surfaces, and deserves its own staging decision rather than being rushed behind this.

- **Verified the complaint before designing:** the modal genuinely renders the type selector **near the bottom**
  (`BookingModal:682`) below the fields it governs, while already carrying `isVoucher` special-casing — so the
  form half-adapts to a choice it asks for last. Backend has `getCourses()`/`getVouchers()` and the summaries
  already carry progress/expiry, but there is **no "who is eligible" answer** anywhere.
- **The design call that matters: eligibility answers on the backend, not in the modal.** The FE could pull all
  courses/vouchers and filter locally, but "**does this student have an *active* course/voucher**" is a **domain
  rule** (not expired, sessions/hours left), and this project's standing rule is backend-as-source-of-truth. A
  second copy in the browser would drift, and it would ship the whole table to answer one question. So
  **`GET /students/eligible?type=…` returns the eligible students *with* the context the modal must show** —
  one call, one definition. I explicitly told Jason: if an "active" helper already exists, **use it, don't
  invent a second**.
- **Tasks cut:** **TASK-051 (@Jason, BE)** — the endpoint + the eligibility predicate (pure-tested: expired ·
  fully used · remaining · multi-course) + the context payload; Trial/Single deliberately keep the normal
  student search, since any student including a brand-new one is valid there. **TASK-052 (@Fern, FE)** — tabs
  first, per-type fields (**Voucher stops asking for teacher/slot** — the existing domain rule, currently
  collected then discarded), eligible lists, compact context, TH/EN.
- **Two reversible design calls I made and flagged rather than buried:** add-new-student is **hidden on the
  Course/Voucher tabs** (it could only create a student who is instantly ineligible), and a student with **two
  active courses appears once per course** so staff choose rather than us guessing.
- **The browser-check rule is in TASK-052's DoD**, phrased to invite the same honesty Fern showed on TASK-049:
  exercise all four tabs, and if the environment blocks a specific interaction, **name it** rather than implying
  coverage.
- **On REQ-023 (not started, deliberately):** Porter's three points are noted — the scope is materially bigger
  than "one morning message", only check (5) depends on REQ-019 (now SPEC_DONE), and ⚠️ **the 08:00 job must
  actually be registered on the server or the feature is silently dead** — which matters because **the two
  existing scheduled jobs have still never been registered**. When I spec it I'll stage it and design so a dead
  job is *visible* (a last-run indicator in the web view) rather than failing silently, since that risk is now
  demonstrated twice over. Flagging the intent now so it isn't a surprise.
- **Nothing waiting on Sober** until 051/052 reach REVIEW. Queue after: REQ-023 → REQ-020 Stage 2 (still needs
  the dual-role answer) → REQ-013 → REQ-021 / REQ-009 / REQ-014.

## [14:20] Jason (BE) — TASK-051 built → REVIEW → @Sober (eligible-students endpoint; unblocks Fern)

Took **TASK-051** ahead of TASK-050 — 051 blocks Fern's TASK-052, and 050 is explicitly marked low / "must not
jump the queue".

- **Reuse (your Question, answered concretely):** vouchers **already had** the rule — **`voucherUsable`** in
  `lib/voucher.ts`, the same helper `prepareVoucherBooking` enforces at booking time — so `voucherEligible`
  **delegates to it** rather than copying, and the list can't drift from what booking accepts. **Courses had no
  equivalent helper**, only the meaning implied by the summary (`expiryDate`/`usedSessions`/`size`), so I added
  `courseEligible` mirroring the same shape in a new pure `lib/eligibility.ts`.
- **Service** `getEligibleStudents(type)` reuses **`getCourses()` / `getVouchers()`** (already embed student +
  subject + summaries) — no new joins. Maps **per entitlement**, so two active courses → **two rows**. Bad/missing
  `type` → **400**. **Route** `GET /students/eligible` registered **before** the other `/students` routes and I

## Sober (SA Lead) — SPEC-018 (REQ-023): daily admin digest
- **Wrote SPEC-018 + TASK-053 (Jason, BE) + TASK-054 (Fern, FE).** REQ-023 → **IN_SPEC**.
- **Correction to the REQ's own analysis, found by reading the schema instead of trusting it: check #5 is
  NOT blocked.** Porter recorded "students with incomplete info needs REQ-019 first" — but REQ-019's fields
  **already landed with TASK-048**: `students.gender` / `birth_date` / `nationality` (`schema.ts:112-115`)
  and `parents.province` (`:80`) are in the DB today. So **all checks are computable now, the REQ has no
  prerequisite**, and คุณฟีน gets the check that prompted her idea in the *first* release. **@Porter: tell her.**
- **Verified Porter's machinery claims myself and they hold:** `notifyAdmins` + `app_settings.line_admin_user_ids`
  (`lib/line-admin.ts`) fan out through the outbox; `routes/internal.ts` gates jobs on `x-internal-secret` with
  a **503 when the secret is unset** (never an open endpoint); `job_runs` exists with a `(job, run_date)` index.
  **No migration, no new plumbing** — this is checks + scheduling only, exactly as he read it.
- **Core design — one registry, one producer, two renderers.** The trap was computing "what needs attention"
  twice (LINE + web panel); they'd drift inside a month. So `lib/attention.ts` holds a check array (adding a
  check = **appending one entry** — that *is* the extensibility, no plugin system), `runAttentionChecks()` is
  the **only** producer, and both the 08:00 job and `GET /api/attention` call it. The panel computes **live**;
  no stored-results table to keep in sync.
- 🔴 **Dead-job visibility, designed in — the thing I actually care about here.** Two scheduled jobs in this
  project have **never been registered** and nobody noticed for weeks, because a job that never runs produces
  nothing to notice. A third would go the same way. So: the job writes a `job_runs` row **even on runs that
  send nothing** ("ran and had nothing to say" must be distinguishable from "never ran"), and TASK-054 renders
  **"⚠️ the daily digest has never run — the 08:00 task is not set up"** on a screen staff open. I also updated
  the standing "Scheduled tasks" blocked row: it's **three** jobs now, register them in one go.
- **Reuse enforced, not suggested:** checks 3–4 must use `lib/eligibility.ts` (TASK-051) for "still active" and
  check 5 the calendar's own freelance `remaining`. A second copy of "active"/"over cap" is the exact drift
  TASK-051 was built to prevent. Also required a **LEFT join** on `incomplete_students` so parentless walk-in
  students don't silently vanish — same failure mode as the badge report.
- **Privacy (REQ-020 lesson):** only **two** checks may name anyone in the LINE message (unconfirmed bookings →
  student+teacher nickname; teachers with no LINE link) — an admin can't act on those otherwise. Everything
  else is a **count in LINE**, names behind login in the panel. Lists truncate at 5. Never a full name, phone,
  DOB or child's name in a chat log on someone's personal phone.
- **Resilience:** one failing check degrades to an entry; the other six still send. A digest that dies because
  one query broke is worse than a partial one.
- **Not staged, deliberately** — nothing is blocked, and BE→FE is two tasks in one delivery, not two stages.
- **Non-blocking question to @Porter:** confirm the thresholds (expiring **14d** · nearly finished **≤2
  sessions** · freelance near cap **≤2h**). They're defaults in one constant block so this ships; I'm not
  waiting on them. 14 days may be too late for a renewal call — that's for คุณฟีน, not me, to say.
- **Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-053** (TASK-054 queued behind it for Fern, who is on TASK-052).

  silence would hide a broken check. One check failing can't kill the digest (per-check try/catch).
- **Privacy:** checks travel in the outbox payload so each admin gets **their own TH/EN**; only the two permitted
  checks name people (nicknames), everything else is a bare count, lists truncate at 5.

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-053 review → REWORK (3 scoped items)
- **Reviewed TASK-053** (attention registry + 08:00 digest + `GET /api/attention`). **Most of it I'd ship as
  is** — the design survived contact with the code: `runAttentionChecks()` really is the only producer (both
  the job and the panel call it, no second path), checks 3–4 really do call `courseEligible`/`voucherEligible`
  and check 5 really does read the same `bo.item.remainingQty` the booking path draws from, and
  `incomplete_students` **LEFT-joins correctly** — parentless walk-in students are kept, which was the failure
  mode I was most worried about. **Ran it myself: `tsc` 0 · `bun test` 215/0.**
- **Two things he did that I hadn't specified and were right:** (a) a **degraded check counts as outstanding**
  in `decideDigest`, so a broken query still sends — silence would hide the breakage, exactly backwards for a
  feature whose job is surfacing problems; (b) the digest payload carries the checks so each admin gets it in
  **their own** TH/EN. I also checked the failure path: `formatOutboxMessage`'s `default` branch returns a
  generic i18n string, **not** a JSON dump, so an unrecognised payload can't spill.
- 🔴 **The one real bug — and it's in the exact mechanism this REQ exists to protect.** `getLastDigestRun()`
  does `.orderBy(jobRuns.runDate).limit(500)` (**ascending**) then `.at(-1)`, so once there are ~500 daily rows
  it returns the **500th-oldest**: the panel would permanently show a last-run date from a year and a half ago
  **while looking perfectly healthy**. A "last ran" widget that silently freezes is worse than none — the whole
  point was that a dead job stops being invisible. Same query is also ambiguous **today**: one `runDate` can
  hold two rows (a clear `sent:false` 08:00 run, then a `sent:true` re-run later). Fix =
  `orderBy(desc(runDate), desc(finishedAt)).limit(1)` + a test that the later row wins.
- 🟡 **Item 2:** the outbox payload carries `items` for **every** check, incl. student nicknames from
  `incomplete_students` that the message deliberately never prints. Nothing leaks today, but **TASK-047 leaked
  precisely because data travelled where it wasn't needed** — enqueue items only for the two `namesPeopleInDigest`
  checks, so the privacy claim doesn't depend on the renderer staying correct forever.
- 🟡 **Item 3 — my spec gap, not his.** `GET /api/attention` returns TH-only titles, which is exactly what I
  wrote; but I also told Fern the titles come from the API, and her panel must be TH+EN. As specced she'd ship
  TH-only or keep a second copy of the labels — the drift this design avoids everywhere else. Asked for
  **`titleKey`** alongside `title`, and **I fixed TASK-054's wording + DoD myself** rather than leaving her to
  discover it. Second spec gap of this kind (after the parent-`note` one in TASK-049) — the pattern is me
  describing an FE contract without checking the BE task actually emits it.
- **Nits, no action:** `isFreelanceNearCap`'s `overLimit(r) ||` clause is subsumed by `r <= 2` and can never
  change the result — fine to keep, just not the reuse that counts (reading the single `remainingQty` source
  is). `studentsWithParent` does two full scans and joins in JS — correct and fine at this size, noted.
- **Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-053 rework** (3 items, nothing else to touch). **TASK-054 stays blocked** so Fern
  builds against the final shape; she's on TASK-052 meanwhile.

## [19:40] Jason (BE) — TASK-053 REWORK applied → REVIEW → @Sober (all 3 items; TASK-054 unblocked)

- **🔴 Item 1 — you were right, and it's the worst kind of bug for this feature.** `orderBy(runDate)` ascending
  + `limit(500)` + `.at(-1)` = the 500th-**oldest** row, so after ~500 daily rows the "last ran" widget would
  freeze on a year-old date **while looking healthy** — the indicator lying in exactly the direction REQ-023
  exists to prevent. Fixed: extracted `lastDigestRunQuery()` with
  **`orderBy(desc(runDate), desc(finishedAt)).limit(1)`**; the scan and `.at(-1)` are gone.
  - **On your "add a test that a later row wins":** a live row-comparison needs a DB (deploy smoke), so I pinned
    the **contract** instead — the query is rendered via Drizzle's **`.toSQL()`** and asserted to order
    `run_date desc` **then** `finished_at desc` (primary key verified by index position), bind `limit 1`, and
    stay scoped to `daily-digest`. It breaks if anyone drops a `desc`, drops the tie-breaker, or restores a wide
    limit. Saying plainly what it does and doesn't prove.
- **🟡 Item 2 — done, and I agree with the reasoning.** The job builds `namesAllowed` from the registry flags
  and enqueues `items` **only** for the two permitted checks (`[]` otherwise), so the persisted outbox row can't
  hold a nickname the message would never print. Privacy now holds at the **data** layer, not just the renderer.
  New test asserts the rule both ways.
- **🟡 Item 3 — done.** Each check now returns `{ key, titleKey, title, count, items }` — `titleKey` for Fern's
  TH+EN, `title` kept as the TH default. **No second copy of the labels.** (Noted it was your spec gap, not a
  defect — no need to flag it as mine either way.)
- **Nits:** kept `overLimit(r) ||` in `isFreelanceNearCap` as an explicit statement that already-over-cap is in
  scope if the threshold is ever lowered to 0 — agreed it isn't the reuse that counts (that's `remainingQty`
  from the one source). Left `studentsWithParent` and the two-checks-one-course overlap exactly as you accepted.
- **Re-verified:** `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bun test` **221/0** (was 215 — +4 query-ordering, +2
  payload-privacy). Nothing else in the task touched.
- **@Sober — TASK-053 back to REVIEW. @Fern: TASK-054 is unblocked** — final shape
  `{ checks: [{key, titleKey, title, count, items}], lastRun: {runDate, finishedAt, sent} | null }`;
  **`lastRun: null` = "never run"**. Still open for BE: **TASK-050** (low).

    DoD you insisted on (enforced in the API, not hidden in the UI), and it held. Good call making it explicit.
  - ❌ **The frontoffice never surfaces that error.** Clicking Save does **nothing visible** — no toast, no

    they are not allowed to book — the failure is only discovered at Save.

  action that is blocked **silently** isn't a working feature — it's an invisible one. This is a **frontoffice
  defect**, not a rethink: the backend is correct and needs no change.
- **@Sober — routing it back to you, with the scope I'd suggest but not prescribe:**
  1. **Surface the API error** on the booking flow (this one and, if the pattern is shared, other `VALIDATION`
     responses — worth checking whether *any* backend error is being swallowed there, since a generic gap would
     be a bigger find than this one instance).
  2. **Don't offer students who can't be booked.** ⚠️ **This is the same design question as REQ-022 Q1** (what to
     do with ineligible students in the picker) — please decide it **once** for both, rather than one behaviour
     in the booking modal and another here.
     - The stakeholder's instinct: **don't list them at all** ("ถ้าระงับ ก็ไม่ควรขึ้นใน list").
     - My counter, for you to weigh: **show them disabled with the reason** — a child silently vanishing from
       search prompts "where did they go?" and a call to the office, whereas "บัญชีผู้ปกครองถูกระงับ" is
       self-explanatory. I've put the choice back to her rather than deciding it myself.

## [evening-2] Porter (PM) — picker decision settled: HIDE suspended (stakeholder's reasoning beat mine) → @Sober

- I proposed showing suspended students **disabled with a reason**; คุณฟีน pushed back with one question:
  **"แล้วเขากดระงับไปทำไม"** — if staff deliberately suspended the account, why would it keep appearing in the
  booking picker at all? **She's right and I'm conceding, not compromising.** Suspension is a deliberate
  decision; the picker should reflect the current decision, not re-litigate it on every booking. My
  "where did the child go?" concern is real but belongs somewhere else — the **People screen**, where the family
  is still fully visible with a suspended state. Cluttering the booking flow to answer a question that has a
  better home was the wrong trade.
- **⇒ DECISION (applies to BOTH REQ-019 and REQ-022 Q1 — decided once, as agreed): students whose parent is
  suspended are NOT listed in the booking student picker.** No disabled row, no placeholder. The People screen is
  where staff see and manage suspended accounts.
- **Two things I want held while implementing this, so "hidden" doesn't become "lost":**
  1. **The server-side block stays regardless.** Hiding is convenience; the `400` is the guarantee. If the picker
     is ever bypassed (stale page, deep link, another entry point), the API must still refuse.
  2. **Error surfacing is still required** — that was the actual defect. Even with the picker filtered, any
     backend rejection must be shown to the user rather than swallowed.
- **One consequence worth stating now so it isn't reported as a bug later:** suspension blocks **new** bookings
  only — sessions already booked before the suspension **remain on the calendar**, by design (we agreed history
  and existing bookings stay untouched). So a suspended family can still be visible on the calendar for
  already-scheduled sessions. If คุณฟีน ever wants those cancelled too, that's a separate decision, not a defect.
- Ball: @Sober (FE fix for REQ-019 with this decision folded in; REQ-022 Q1 now answered too).

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-052 review → REWORK (a free-session leak) + TASK-055 raised + my spec corrected
- **Reviewed TASK-052.** The modal itself is right — type-first tabs, per-tab fields, eligible lists, context
  straight from TASK-051 with no FE recompute, and **item 7 wired properly** (the backend's own message surfaces
  in a red alert by the date). Her browser check is **honest**: all four tabs exercised for real, and she stated
  plainly that the live submit + the expiry rejection weren't triggered because the mock doesn't enforce expiry.
  `bunx tsc --noEmit` → 0 (my run).
- 🔴 **But the Course tab books a session that draws down nothing.** The payload sends
  `bookingType: "COURSE_PACKAGE"` with **no `courseId`** — while `ctx.courseId` sits right there. Traced it:
  `insertBooking` stores `courseId ?? null`; the check-in increment (`scheduler.service.ts:786`) and the
  end-of-day NO_SHOW cut are **both gated on `courseId`**. So the session is **free either way** — attended it
  never increments `usedSessions`, no-showed it's never cut — and the course's remaining count, **the number
  TASK-051's eligibility reads**, drifts from reality. It also makes "two active courses → staff pick which"
  decorative: the pick is thrown away at submit.
  - **And it needs no BE change**: `validation.ts:83` already has `courseId: ID.optional()`. What's missing is
    `courseId?: string` on the **FE's own** `CreateBookingInput`, which is why it looked unsupported. ~3 lines.
  - Lesson I passed back: "payload unchanged" was true of our client type, **not of the API** — check the server
    before concluding a field isn't supported.
- 🟡 Second item: the voucher alert says "it uses this slot" (honest, good) but doesn't **name** the teacher/time
  it will use. `createSlot` has both. Staff shouldn't have to remember which column they clicked — that's the
  "less staff memory" goal of the whole REQ.
- **✅ Her flag 1 was right and I was wrong — my spec error, owned.** SPEC-017 told her "Voucher: no teacher, no
  fixed slot — **the existing domain rule**". It isn't. `scheduler.service.ts:569-571` says outright: *"No
  teacher restriction here — 'can't pick a teacher' is a **purchase-time** rule, not a per-session one."* A
  voucher session lands in a real slot with a real teacher; what it lacks is the **recurring locked** slot a
  course has. **I asserted a domain rule without reading the code — exactly what I tell the engineers not to
  do**, and she caught it by reading. Her resolution (take teacher/time from the clicked slot, don't ask) stays.
  **SPEC-017 corrected in place** — both the voucher rule and a clarification that "payload unchanged" ≠ "send
  fewer fields".
- **Raised TASK-055 (BE, small):** `validation.ts` refuses a VOUCHER booking with no `voucherId` (*"ไม่งั้น
  ชั่วโมงจะไม่ถูกตัด"*) but has **no symmetric rule for COURSE_PACKAGE** — which is why an FE slip could mint a
  free session at all. The UI must not be the only defence; same reasoning as the server-side suspend gate. Told
  Jason to **verify no other caller depends on the leniency and stop if one does**, rather than force it.
- **Ball:** **@Fern — TASK-052 rework (2 items, re-check the Course tab submit in the browser)**;
  **@Jason — TASK-055** (small) and TASK-050 (low). **@Porter** still owes the REQ-023 deploy + 08:00 task
  registration, the dual-role answer, the จังหวัด badge question, and REQ-019 acceptance.

  **generic** catch on booking submit: `if (e instanceof ApiClientError) setSubmitError(e.message)`. It isn't
  voucher-specific — it surfaces **any** backend rejection, including the suspend `400
  VALIDATION "บัญชีผู้ปกครองถูกระงับ…"`. The acceptance ran against a build **without** TASK-052. So this needs
  **no new work at all**, only the deploy that's already pending. That also answers Porter's side-question
  ("does the booking flow swallow *all* backend errors?") — it did; it doesn't any more.
- **Defect (2) — the picker — is a BACKEND fix, not an FE one.** "Is this household suspended" is a domain rule
  that already exists server-side (`lib/suspend.ts`, enforced in `insertBooking`). Filtering it in the browser
  would put a second copy where it will drift — the same argument as TASK-051's eligibility. So the pickers must
  stop returning them.

  (`scheduler.service.ts:362`) filters entitlement only, and `searchStudents` (`parent.service.ts:316`) has no
  suspension clause at all. **All four booking tabs are affected** — so this closes REQ-019's blocker *and* a
  gap we would otherwise have shipped brand-new in REQ-022.
- ⚠️ **The trap I avoided, and the reason this took tracing rather than one line:** `GET /students?q=` is **not
  only** the booking picker — `StudentSelect` also backs **CreateCourseModal** and **CreateVoucherModal**, i.e.
  *selling*. Blanket-filtering the shared endpoint would silently change two screens **nobody decided about**
  (selling ≠ booking; a voucher sale creates no booking at all). So **TASK-056** filters `/students/eligible`
  unconditionally — it exists only to answer "who can be booked" — and adds an **opt-in flag** to `/students`,
  leaving today's default untouched. A one-line "filter suspended everywhere" would have been wrong.

  voucher at all?** Today they can, and I've deliberately **not** changed it — that's a business call, not mine.

- **TASK-056 is ahead of TASK-055 and TASK-050** — it's the only thing standing between REQ-019 and delivered.

## [21:30] Jason (BE) — TASK-056 + TASK-055 built → REVIEW → @Sober

Took them in the order the board set: **056 first** (the REQ-019 acceptance blocker), then **055** ("do it with
your next BE work"). Both in one pass since they touch adjacent code.

**TASK-056 — suspended households out of the booking pickers.** Flag name: **`bookable=true`**.
- **One helper, one rule:** `suspendedStudentIds()` builds the exclusion set by filtering joined rows through
  **`isSuspended` from `lib/suspend.ts`** — both pickers consume it, neither restates the rule.
- **The `innerJoin` in that helper is deliberate** (and commented): it builds the set to **exclude**, so a
  parentless walk-in student never appears in it and **stays visible**. Excluding **by student id** also
  sidesteps the SQL-NULL trap — `parent_id NOT IN (…)` is NULL for parentless rows and would have silently
  deleted the whole walk-in cohort. Avoided **by construction**, not by remembering to LEFT-join.
- `/students/eligible` filtered **unconditionally** (both types); `/students` filtered **only** with the flag —
  Zod defaults it to `false` and rejects anything that isn't `"true"`/`"false"`, so the course/voucher **sale**
  modals can't change by accident. **No consumer of `GET /students` that you missed** — I re-grepped; it's the
  booking picker + the two sale modals, exactly as you described. FE passing the flag is your follow-up, not
  done here.

**TASK-055 — `courseId` required on COURSE_PACKAGE.** Symmetric `.refine` next to the voucher one, with the
consequence written in the comment.
- **I verified the caller question myself rather than taking your word, and it matches:** `v.createBooking` is
  used by **exactly one** route (`POST /bookings`); `createCoursePackage` calls `insertBooking` **directly**
  (`:632`, validated by a different schema, always has a courseId) and the sick-leave auto-extension inserts
  directly (`:849`, no route validation). **No legitimate path posts a course booking without a course**, so
  there was nothing to stop and report.

- **Verify (both):** `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bun test` **232/0** (was 221 — +6 picker semantics incl. the
  parentless and no-flag guarantees, +5 refine branches incl. a symmetry check on the voucher rule). ✅ Also
  checked the flag contract directly: no flag → `false`, `=false` → `false`, `=true` → `true`, `=yes` →
  **rejected**. DB-backed picker results remain deploy smoke; steps in the task notes.
- **@Sober — TASK-056 + TASK-055 → REVIEW.** Remaining BE: **TASK-050** (low).

`path: ["courseId"]`, and the comment states the *consequence* rather than restating the code. He did the caller
check properly instead of asserting it: `v.createBooking` has exactly one route, and the two other
booking-creating paths (`createCoursePackage` → `insertBooking` directly, sick-leave auto-extension → direct
insert) **bypass the schema**, each named with why. His **symmetry test** — asserting the voucher rule still
holds both ways — guards the backstop against the very failure the backstop exists for.

**TASK-056 (suspended pickers) → DONE, and his answer is better than my instruction.** I told him to keep the
LEFT join so walk-in students survive. He inverted it instead: build the set of students to **exclude** via an
`innerJoin`, so a parentless student **can't be in it by construction** — and pointed out that the obvious
`parent_id NOT IN (…)` would evaluate to **NULL** for parentless rows and silently delete the entire walk-in
cohort. That's the badge-report failure mode avoided by structure rather than by remembering a rule, and he
commented it so nobody "fixes" it back.
- **The thing I checked first and he got right:** the empty-set case. `notInArray(col, [])` is the classic way
  this pattern returns nothing on the happy path (no suspended households at all) — he guards with
  `excluded.length ? and(...) : searchWhere`.
- **The opt-in contract is closed properly:** `bookable` defaults to off and `bookable=yes` is **rejected**, so
  the course/voucher **sale** screens can't lose students to a typo'd query string. He tested that contract
  directly instead of trusting Zod's coercion.
- **Accepted with a note, no action:** the helper reads students⋈parents per call, including each debounced
  keystroke. A `NOT EXISTS` clause would avoid the second query — but would restate the suspend rule in SQL,
  which is exactly what I forbade. He made the tradeoff I asked for; fine at this size.

**🔴 Deploy-order hazard I found while reviewing — this is the one thing in the batch that can break prod.**
TASK-055 makes `POST /bookings` **reject** a COURSE_PACKAGE booking with no `courseId`, and the FE only started
sending it in TASK-052. **Deploy the frontoffice FIRST (or both together); backend-first 400s every Course-tab
booking.** FE-first is safe (old backend has always accepted the field as optional). Put in the task, the board
row, and flagged to @Porter for the manifest — it's the batch's only ordering constraint.

**Raised TASK-057 (Fern, small):** the booking picker must actually pass `bookable=true`, or the Trial/Single
tabs still list suspended households — the backend flag is opt-in by design. Called out the one way it can go
wrong: **the flag must be part of the react-query key**, or a cached sale-modal result gets reused in the
booking picker. The Course/Voucher tabs need **no** FE change (`/students/eligible` filters unconditionally).

**Ball:** **@Fern — TASK-057** (then TASK-054). **@Porter — the deploy-order line above**, plus the REQ-022 and
REQ-023 deploys + the 08:00 task registration, and the "can a suspended family BUY a course/voucher?" question.
**@Jason — TASK-050** (low) is all that's left. **Nothing waiting on Sober**; next SA work is **REQ-009**.

## Sober (SA Lead) — sell-side suspend block scoped (TASK-058 + TASK-059); retiring my own `bookable` flag
Picked up the one thing left on me. คุณฟีน's answer — **"ไม่ควรซื้อได้"** — completes the definition of
"suspended", so I wrote it down as one sentence in a **SPEC-016 addendum** rather than leaving it spread across
a log, a REQ file and a board cell: *no LINE bot · no new bookings · **no purchase** · existing bookings and
entitlements untouched · fully visible to staff on the People screen.*

**Verified as-built before scoping anything:**
- **`createVoucher` has no suspend gate at all** — a suspended family can buy a voucher today, and the sale even
  posts revenue (`recordSale`) afterwards.
- **`createCoursePackage` only fails *incidentally*** — it creates sessions, and `insertBooking`'s **booking**
  gate rejects them, so the transaction rolls back. The outcome is right today, but **incidental enforcement is
  not enforcement**: it breaks the moment someone reorders that code or adds a course type that books no
  sessions. TASK-058 makes it explicit, gated **after `resolveStudentId`** (that call can find-or-create the
  household) and **before the revenue post**.

**⚠️ I'm retiring the `bookable` flag I introduced this morning — and I want the reasoning on the record, not
just the reversal.** I built it (TASK-056) *because* the sell-side question was open, and blanket-filtering a
shared endpoint would have silently changed two screens nobody had decided about. That was right with the
information available. Now that all three consumers — booking picker and **both** sale modals — want the same
thing, an opt-in policy flag means *"remember to ask for the policy"*, and whoever forgets opens a silent hole.
**Defaults must be the safe answer**, so exclusion becomes the default and the flag goes. I am deliberately
**not** adding an `includeSuspended` escape hatch: nothing needs one (the People screen reads `/parents`), and
I won't build a lever on speculation.
- **No deploy-order hazard this time, and I'm saying so explicitly** — an old FE still sending `bookable=true`
  is *ignored* by the new schema, not rejected. I made noise about ordering on TASK-055 and don't want that
  alarm inherited where it doesn't apply.
- **The churn is mine, not Fern's** — TASK-059 says so. She wired the flag correctly one round before the
  answer landed.

**Second defect folded into TASK-059:** once the sale paths start returning the suspension `400`,
`CreateCourseModal` / `CreateVoucherModal` would show a Save button that appears to do nothing — **exactly the
defect REQ-019's acceptance failed on**, in two screens that never got TASK-052's `ApiClientError` fix. Hiding
the students is convenience; the message is what staff actually need when it happens.

**Out of scope, stated so nobody assumes it:** top-ups / extensions of an existing entitlement are **not**
"buying" here. Separate small REQ if คุณฟีน wants those blocked too — @Porter, worth asking when convenient,
not now.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-058** (then TASK-050, low). **@Fern — TASK-054 first, then TASK-059.** @Porter — the
🔴 **08:00 task registration** is still the one thing that would leave REQ-023 silently dead; the stakeholder
deferred it until TASK-054 lands, which is fine as long as it isn't forgotten. **Nothing waiting on Sober**;
next SA work is **REQ-009**.

**TASK-054 (needs-attention panel) → DONE.**
- She treated the **last-run indicator as the point of the panel**, which is why I wrote the task: three states,
  all visibly distinct — never-run (red), sent, and **"ran but nothing was outstanding"**. That third one is the
  whole reason the job writes a `job_runs` row even when it sends nothing; without it, "quiet" and "dead" look
  identical. **And she exercised the never-run state for real** via a mock flag rather than reasoning about it —

  (`count: null`) renders in its own group, so a broken check can't hide inside a calm-looking screen.

  `title`**, so a future eighth check added server-side still shows a Thai label instead of a raw key. The
  registry's extensibility survives into the UI — I hadn't asked for that.
- Answered her Question: keying by the stable `key` (not the literal `att_<key>`) is right — the `att_` prefix
  is an internal detail of the backend's i18n table and shouldn't leak into the FE dictionary.
- Left one **non-rework** note: the FE types `finishedAt` as non-null while the API types it nullable
  (`job_runs.finished_at` is nullable), so a row without it would render "Invalid Date". Unreachable today.

## Sober (SA Lead) — SPEC-020 written for REQ-013 (SOM dashboard)
Nothing waiting on me and Jason was down to a LOW task, so I took the next REQ. **REQ-013 is the one REQ-019's
demographics were collected for**, so it's the natural next.

**Two things the REQ believed that are no longer true — both found by reading, not assuming:**
- **✅ Metric 4 is not blocked.** The REQ says demographics "depend on REQ-012". REQ-012 was **superseded**, and
  the fields shipped with **REQ-019/TASK-048** — now DELIVERED. **All five metrics are computable today**, so
  there is nothing to stage.
- **✅ Metric 5 is already built.** `getDailyReport` (`:496`) already returns `totalBooked` vs `attended` and is
  already served at `/reports/daily`. That *is* expected-vs-attended. I told Jason to **reuse it verbatim** —
  writing a second daily count would be the exact drift I keep pushing back on.

**Design: one endpoint, one snapshot.** `GET /api/reports/som` returns all five sections together, because they
are read together and must describe the same instant — five endpoints could straddle a booking and show a
dashboard that disagrees with itself. **No parameters:** "today" and "this month" are resolved in Bangkok time
server-side, so two staff at a month boundary can't see different months and each conclude the report is broken.
Same "one producer" shape as `runAttentionChecks`, so the codebase has one idea of what a report is.

**🔴 The part I care most about — unknowns are a first-class category, not a filtered-out remainder.**
Every breakdown LEFT-joins with an explicit `unknown` bucket (walk-in students have `parent_id = null`
**by design**; gender/DOB/nationality are **all optional**), **and** each carries `{known, unknown, total}` that
**the FE must display**. The reason is timing: right after launch **most demographics will be blank**, so a
clean pie chart over 12 of 48 students is accurate and completely misleading at the same time — and that's the
kind of number that ends up in someone's decision. A dashboard that admits its gap beats one that looks
confident. The gap is already actionable elsewhere: REQ-023's `incomplete_students` check names exactly who is
missing data. **The dashboard shows the hole; the digest chases it** — no duplication.

**On the REQ's three open questions — I answered two of them myself rather than spending คุณฟีน's attention.**
*Mixed-sport students* → **primary sport**: it's Porter's recommendation and also the only option where the
shares sum to 100% (counting every sport a student does silently answers a different question). *Un-started
vouchers* → **counted as current**, which is simply what `voucherEligible` already says, so we inherit the
answer instead of minting a rule. For *"new this month"* I'm **showing both** first-trial and registration
separately — they answer different questions (reach vs conversion), the data is right there, and picking one
would throw the other away for no saving. Only if คุณปุ้ม wants a single headline number does she need to choose.

**One definitional call I flagged rather than buried:** "existing customer with a course" reuses `courseEligible`
= **not expired AND sessions remaining**, so a fully-used-but-unexpired course doesn't count. I think that's
right (nothing left to book) and the renewal segment is already surfaced by REQ-023's `nearly_finished_courses`
— but it's her definition of "customer", not mine. Non-blocking, one predicate.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-062** (TASK-050 still low). **@Fern — TASK-061**, then TASK-063. **@Porter — the
deploy** on คุณฟีน's timing, incl. the 08:00 registration and REQ-023's panel-based acceptance. **Nothing
waiting on Sober**; REQ-021 (badges) and REQ-014 (revenue by activity) are next in my queue.

## [01:20] Jason (BE) — TASK-062 built → REVIEW → @Sober (SOM dashboard snapshot)

- **Nothing was missing** — all five sections come from existing fields, your schema check held.
- **I took the "pure function over rows, not clever SQL" instruction literally**, so every rule is unit-tested
  without a DB: `breakdown` (blank/null/undefined → `unknown`, bucket **always emitted even at 0**),
  `ageFrom`/`ageBand` (derived at read time; **no DOB → unknown, not age 0**), `primarySport` (most bookings,
  tie → most recent; **zero bookings → null**, so a new student can't break the share), `monthOf`/`inMonth`.
- **Reuse:** (a)/(b) use `courseEligible`/`voucherEligible` — which, as you said, also settles the un-started
  voucher question by inheritance — and `today` is **`getDailyReport` verbatim**. No second definition of
  "active" or "attended".
- **⚠️ Caught a real bug while writing it, worth naming:** my first `inMonth` compared `toISOString()` — **UTC**.
  A course bought 01 Aug 02:00 Bangkok is 31 Jul 19:00 UTC, so **for the first 7 hours of every month** the
  new/renewing counts would have been filed under the previous month. Now shifted +07:00 (no DST → exact) with a
  test pinning both sides of the boundary. Exactly the failure your "no params, resolve server-side" rule exists
  to prevent — it just moved from the request into my arithmetic.
- **Unknowns:** `province` buckets over a JS **LEFT join** so a parentless walk-in lands in `unknown` instead of
  vanishing; every breakdown carries `{known, unknown, total}` for "based on X of Y".
- **Verify:** `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bun test` **255/0** (+16 covering exactly your DoD list). ✅ Also
  assembled the breakdowns over representative rows (incl. a walk-in and a zero-booking student) and asserted in
  real output: shares **sum to the student count**, a two-sport student **counted once**, walk-in in
  `unknown` province, coverage "2 of 4".
- **Maintenance (3rd time, flagging the pattern only):** the new `getDailyReport` import meant the whole-module
  stub in `api.teacher-routing.test.ts` needed that export too, or the ESM link fails. If it recurs, narrowing

**TASK-062 → DONE — and the best thing in it is a bug he caught in his own code.**
`inMonth` originally compared `toISOString()`, i.e. **UTC**. A course bought 01 Aug 02:00 Bangkok is 31 Jul
19:00 UTC, so **for the first 7 hours of every month** the new/renewing counts would have filed under the
previous month — exactly when someone opens the dashboard to see how the month started. **Silent, periodic, and
self-healing by lunchtime**, which is the hardest kind of defect to ever get reported: by the time anyone
double-checks, the number is right. He found it, named the mechanism, fixed it with a fixed +07:00 offset
(correct — Thailand has no DST) and pinned **both sides** of the boundary with tests. My "no parameters, resolve
server-side" rule prevents the two-staff-disagree version of this; it does **not** prevent this one. He found
the version the rule doesn't cover.

Also verified rather than trusted: the `unknown` bucket is pushed **even at 0** (so the FE never infers its
absence) and blank strings trim into unknown; the province LEFT-join is real (`{student, parent: null}`), so
walk-ins land in `unknown` instead of vanishing — the condition I attached when province moved to the parent is
now true in code; and **`ageFrom` returns `null`, not 0**, for a missing DOB. That last one matters more than it
looks: age 0 would have invented a cohort of infants out of missing data, which is precisely how a dashboard
lies while every individual number is technically real.

**One thing I took onto my own side rather than sending back:** `breakdown` hardcodes the unknown bucket's label
as `"ไม่ระบุ"`, so an English dashboard would show Thai. Not a defect — the bucket has a stable **`key`** — so I
added an instruction to **TASK-063** to label it from Fern's own dictionary. Same split as the `titleKey` gap I
owned on TASK-053: **the API supplies identity, the FE supplies language.** Cheaper to fix in the task I was
about to hand over than to bounce a correct endpoint.

## Sober (SA Lead) — SPEC-021 written for REQ-014 (revenue by activity + customer spend)
Queue check first: **REQ-021 (badges) is explicitly parked** — "BACKLOG, LOWEST, do not start without the
stakeholder raising it". So I skipped it rather than spending a session on work she has said she doesn't want
yet. Next real item was REQ-014.

**The mechanism, found by reading rather than assuming.** Every sale is a `bo.movement` with
`refType: "SALE"`. Two fields identify it: the item's **product code** says *what kind*, and **`refId`** says
*which row* — `course-{size}`→`course_packages.id`, `first-trial`/`single-session`→`bookings.id`,
`voucher-{hours}`→`vouchers.id`. `bo.movement` itself carries neither subject nor student, but both are one hop
away.

**Design: derive by joining.** The REQ offered "tag each sale with its sport" or "per-subject income items" —
**neither**. A read-side attribution map `(code, refId) → {studentId, subjectId}` needs **no migration**, works
**retroactively on every historical sale** (which tagging-going-forward can never do), and can't drift because
it's derived at read time. Per-subject income items would multiply the catalogue by the number of sports and
re-open the price model.
**One map, both features** — grouped by subject for revenue, by student for spend. Building it twice is exactly
how two screens end up disagreeing about one month's total.

**backoffice-back will read `public` directly, read-only.** One database since REQ-006, so it's a plain join;
I wrote the ownership rule into the task and asked for it as a **comment at the declaration site**:
backoffice-back **reads and never writes or migrates** `public`. This mirrors the direction REQ-006 already
chose (scheduling-back touches `bo.item` directly rather than over HTTP), so it's the established pattern here,
not a new liberty. An HTTP hop would add a failure mode and a second copy of the rule for nothing.

**🔴 The finding that shapes the whole report: a voucher is structurally unattributable.** It's generic hours —
no sport at sale, and its sessions may later be *different* sports. No join fixes that and no amount of tagging
at sale time would be honest. So the report carries an explicit **`unattributed`** bucket, and I made
**`buckets + unattributed === totalMinor` a tested identity**. Same principle as SPEC-020's `unknown`, but the
stakes are higher: a finance report that silently drops what it can't classify shows a tidy split that
**doesn't add up to the month's real revenue**, and that's the number an executive acts on. I also told Fern the
percentages must be of the **total**, not of the attributed subset — otherwise every sport is quietly inflated.

**Carried TASK-062's lesson across explicitly.** Jason found the UTC month-boundary bug there; this report is
month-bucketed **and financial**, so the same mistake would move *revenue* between months. Same +07:00 fix, same
both-sides test, written into the DoD.

**Two things routed, one deliberately not.** The REQ's "method" question I answered myself — it's a design call
with a clearly better option, not a business one. What I **did** route: **when should voucher revenue belong to
a sport?** Recognising it per session as consumed changes *when* revenue is recognised, which is คุณปุ้ม's call,
not mine — non-blocking, the report ships with the honest bucket either way, and it's best asked with the number
in front of her ("vouchers were ฿X last month"). And an **FYI I want ahead of a disappointment**: this delivers
revenue by **sport only** — "ยอดตามสาขา / onsite-vs-online" is still structurally impossible (REQ-021 finding 9),
so @Porter should check that isn't what she's expecting from this.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-064** (backoffice-back :4010, *not* the scheduling API). **@Fern — TASK-063** in
flight, then TASK-065 (backoffice-front :3018). **@Porter — the deploy batch** + the 08:00 registration +
REQ-023's panel acceptance, and the voucher-attribution question when convenient. **Nothing waiting on Sober** —
with REQ-021 parked, my READY_FOR_SA queue is now empty.

## Sober (SA Lead) — 🔴 Jason blocked TASK-064 and was right: sales have not been recorded since 2026-07-28
He stopped before writing a line of code and brought me a premise check. **Best catch of the day**, and I
verified both load-bearing claims myself rather than accepting them: `catalog` is mounted **nowhere** in
backoffice-back's `api.ts`/`index.ts` (so `recordSale`'s POST 404s), and `bo.item` has `ownerRef` +
`externalSource` and **no `externalRef`** (`schema.ts:479-500`).

**Two of the three breaks are my errors, and I've recorded them as mine in SPEC-021, the task and the board:**
1. **I invented `bo.item.externalRef`.** I read `recordSale(externalRef, …)` in `ops-client.ts` and assumed the
   parameter named its *destination* column. It names the **ops** `catalog_items` key — a schema we retired.
   **I asserted a column from a function signature instead of opening the schema**, which is precisely what I
   tell the engineers not to do, and what I criticised Fern for two rounds ago ("check the server contract, not
   our own types"). Same mistake, one level up.
2. **"Retroactive coverage — every historical sale is attributed too" is not achievable**, and I used it as a
   *reason to prefer* the design. Repair attributes sales **going forward**; the past holds only the one-shot
   backfill's residue, which can't include courses or vouchers because **those INCOME items never existed**.
   Asked @Porter to correct it **before คุณปุ้ม hears it** — an unmet promise about money is the worst kind to
   let travel.

**What he found is bigger than this REQ.** Both `recordSale` call sites are `void recordSale(...)` — best-effort
**by design** — so a 404 produced no error, no log, no symptom. **No sale has been recorded since the REQ-006
rebuild (TASK-027, deployed 2026-07-28)** and nobody noticed, including me, while I specced a report on top of
it. His sharpest line is the one I'd want quoted back at me: built as specced, the report returns **฿0 with the
sum check passing** (0+0===0) — my `unattributed` rule stops us dropping what we **can't classify**, but nothing
in it stopped us reporting nothing **to** classify.

**This is the third instance of one failure mode this week** — two scheduled jobs never registered, a digest
that would have died silently, and now revenue quietly not posting. It isn't bad luck: **anything that can stop
without saying so eventually will.**

**Rulings:**
- **Q1 → hold (a).** Not close. A finance report reading ฿0 is wrong in the one direction nobody can catch by
  looking at it. TASK-064 BLOCKED, TASK-065 behind it; Fern has TASK-063 and isn't idle.
- **Q2 → add `external_ref` to `bo.item`.** `ownerRef` already means *teacher id* on freelance items;
  overloading it gives one column two meanings distinguished only by which query you happen to be reading.
  `bo` is ours outright, so it breaks no ownership rule — hand-authored + journal-registered per TASK-042.
- **Q3 → routed to @Porter** as a DATA REQUEST (2 read-only counts; I can't run SQL either).

**Cut the work rather than leaving it as a finding:**
- **TASK-066** — repair the write path: direct Drizzle `bo.movement` write like the freelance ceiling already
  does (killing the last dependency on the retired `ops` HTTP hop), the `external_ref` migration, the missing
  `course-*`/`voucher-*` INCOME items, and **loud** failure logging. I told him to seed course/voucher prices as
  **clearly-marked placeholders** and say so — *a wrong price that looks right is worse than an obvious
  placeholder* — and **not** to backfill the missing days on his own initiative, because re-posting historical
  revenue is a finance decision, not a repair. Both routed to Porter/คุณปุ้ม on the board.
- **TASK-067** — an **8th attention check** (`sales_not_posted`), so the detector lives where someone already
  looks every morning. A log line is only read by someone already looking. It also **tests SPEC-018's own
  claim** that adding a check is one array entry — and I told him that if it needs more, *that's a finding about
  my spec, not a problem with the task.*

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-066 reviewed → DONE. And the hole is far bigger than I said.
Verified in both repos myself: scheduling `tsc` 0 / **275 pass 0 fail**; backoffice `tsc` 0 / **48 pass 0 fail**;
`drizzle/*.sql` = 6 and `_journal.json` = 6 tags.

**🔴 His escalation corrects my framing, and the correction matters more than the fix.** I said "no sales since
2026-07-28". That's true for **two** of the eight product codes. For the other six —
**`course-4/6/10` and `voucher-5/10/15` — the INCOME items never existed in any schema**, so a by-ref post for
them always 404'd. **Those two product families have never had a single baht recorded, in all history**, and the
P&L's income side has been trial/single only. The unmounted route hid an older bug behind it. He's right that
Porter must ask it in exactly those words: *"five days of trial/single revenue"* and *"all course and voucher
revenue, ever"* are different conversations, and only one of them is alarming.

**The sign rule I checked independently, because it's silent money.** `bo-money.ts:18` is
`-qty × unitPriceMinor`; his `saleMovement` produces `qty = -1` with a **positive** value. They agree. Backwards,
every sale would have **subtracted** from the month and the P&L would still have looked plausible. Extracting it
as a pure, unit-tested function instead of trusting it inline was the right instinct — and I re-derived it from
the backoffice side rather than reading his and nodding, because "it matches" is exactly the kind of claim that
is easy to accept and expensive to be wrong about.

**The real fix isn't the plumbing, it's `lib/sale-items.ts`.** The product codes were spelled in **three** places
with nothing tying any of them to an item — *that* is how six codes could exist with nothing to post to and
nobody notice for months. One list now feeds both the sale path and the ensure-items script, and his test
asserting **every code the service can emit has an item** is the one that would have caught this at the start.

**Answered his placeholder-price question: keep them, don't ship with no items.** Shipping without items means
every course/voucher sale logs an error and posts nothing — the current broken state with better logging.
**A visible wrong number gets corrected; an absent one gets rediscovered in three months.** His derivation is
defensible (฿1,390 × hours from an already-approved live placeholder, pinned by a test so it can't drift into
looking like a real price list), he refused `0` for the right reason (a ฿0 sale is the same silent nothing in a
new costume), and `metadata.pricePlaceholder: true` makes them findable **in the data**. His own warning that
the 6-/10-session prices are almost certainly too high (no bulk discount) goes to Porter with the numbers.

**Ruled on his two flags, neither of which is his to carry:** `lib/ops-client.ts` is now **entirely dead code**
(four functions, no callers) — correct not to delete it unasked; recorded as tech debt. And backoffice-back's
snapshot chain is **also** incomplete with **no** README warning — he was right that inventing a repo convention
unasked wasn't his call, **so I asked for it**: add the same warning file on his next pass there.

**What I take from today.** Three silent stoppages in one week — unregistered jobs, a digest that would have
died quietly, and revenue that never posted at all. Every one of them was *invisible by design*: a `void`
call, an unregistered task, a best-effort write. The pattern isn't carelessness, it's that **we keep building
things whose failure mode is silence**. TASK-067 is the direct answer for sales; the attention panel is the
answer for jobs. I'd rather over-invest there than find a fourth.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-067** (it now has something real to watch), then TASK-064 resumes on `external_ref`,
then TASK-050. **@Porter — the escalation above is the important one**: course/voucher revenue has *never* been
recorded, real prices are needed, and the re-post decision + DATA REQUEST are still open. **@Fern — TASK-063**
in flight. **Nothing waiting on Sober.**

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-067 reviewed → DONE. SPEC-018 amended by measurement.
Verified myself: `tsc` 0, `bun test` **280/0**, registry is 8 entries, and the digest renderer, `decideDigest`,
the job, the runner, `getAttention` and the endpoint are all untouched.

**He answered the extensibility question honestly instead of protecting my slogan.** The accurate claim is
**"one array entry, plus one `ctx.load` field + loader if the check needs a source nobody has loaded yet"** — he
could have quietly added the loader and reported "one entry, as promised", and I would not have noticed.
**I've amended SPEC-018 in place and credited the measurement**, because the next person reads the spec, not
this task. His reasoning for *why* the loader was needed is right and not a concession: keeping
`lib/attention.ts` free of query plumbing is what makes the predicates pure and testable, and importing `db`
into it to preserve a tidier sentence would have been the wrong trade.

**Three design calls I'd have wanted and didn't have to ask for:** absence of a movement is the *whole* signal
(no status flag ⇒ no second source of truth about the same fact, and it can't disagree with what TASK-066
writes); only **ATTENDED** trial/single count, because that's when their revenue is recognised — a coarser check
would cry wolf every morning until someone muted it; and the window constant sits in the existing threshold
block so the loader and the check can't drift apart.

**Accepted his 7-day window imprecision, and his reasoning is better than exactness.** It can reach back up to
7h further than 7×24h at the boundary — deliberate, because **a detector that over-includes shows something a
few hours early, while one that under-includes hides the fault it exists to find.** Nothing here is bucketed by
month and no money is attributed, so it isn't TASK-062's bug in a new hat. Stating it is what makes it a
decision rather than a bug.

**The unasked guard is the best thing in the task.** He asserted that every check's `titleKey` is `att_<key>`
**and resolves in both TH and EN** — because `t()` returns the key itself when missing. That is **the exact gap
I owned on TASK-053**, turned into a failing test. I fixed it once by hand; he made it unrepeatable. That's the
difference between correcting a mistake and closing it, and it's the second time today an engineer has hardened
one of my own slips rather than just working around it.

The system holds **one price per package size**. The real list is priced **per program × package**:

| Program | 6 HRS |
|---|---|
| Bike / Skate family | 6,490 |
| Onewheel | 7,990 |
| Balance Play (private) | 7,490 |
| Balance Play (group) | 5,290 |

One `course-6` cannot hold four prices. Same at 10 h and for a 1-hour session (1,090 / 1,390 / 1,690).

**2. 🔴 And this hits REQ-014 directly: "revenue by activity (sport)" is unbuildable if a sale never records
which program it was.** That is the REQ in flight. Jason blocked TASK-064 because SPEC-021's input didn't exist;
this is a **second, independent way the same input is missing** — even with the write path repaired, the *item*
carries no program. Worth checking whether your `external_ref` design already carries it; if it does, say so and
I'll stop worrying. **Design is yours — I'm establishing the requirement, not the solution.**

**3. The placeholders were wrong in a specific, informative direction.** Every one **overstates** revenue, and
the error **grows with package size**: `course-4` +16 %, `course-6` +29 %, **`course-10` +42 %**, `voucher-15`
**+54 %**. `first-trial` at ฿1,390 is **exactly right**. Jason predicted this precisely — he warned his figures
assumed no bulk discount and were "likely too high for 6/10". The real card shows why: the per-hour rate
**falls** with size (1,198 → 1,082 → 979), which a flat hourly rate structurally cannot express. **His refusal
to ship ฿0, and the `pricePlaceholder: true` marker, are what make these rows findable and correctable now.**

  offers 4/6/10 to every program ⇒ **staff can sell a package that does not exist.**
- **Vouchers are excluded from Onewheel and Balance Play** on the card. Nothing in the system knows this.
- **Equipment rental** (200 / 150 / 50 / 50 per hour) is real income with **no representation at all**.

  be *derived* from these. I'm flagging this loudly because gross-vs-net is the kind of assumption that gets made
  silently in a pricing constant and then quietly misstates every report built on top of it.

**2. 🔴 A requirement nobody had scoped, and it gates go-live: REQ-025 (written, DRAFT).**
The stakeholder raised it while answering the backfill question:
> *"ถ้าเปิดระบบ แล้ว ระบบ frontoffice มันต้องรับข้อมูล คอร์สที่มันกำลังเดินอยู่ มาระบบเรา จาก เดิม เป็น excel"*

On launch day the school **does not start from zero.** Children are mid-course *right now* — bought 10 sessions
in Excel, attended 4, **6 remain**. Those courses keep running across the switchover.

**Why this is sharper than it first sounds, and why it lands on you rather than in a backlog:**
- If the system starts empty, every one of those children is a **new customer with no entitlement** on day one.
  Staff would have to re-sell a course the family already paid for, or book outside any course — **which is
  exactly the "free sessions" hole TASK-052/055 closed.** Our safest state becomes unusable on the first morning.
- **The obvious shortcut is a trap.** "Just sell them all again at go-live" is the natural move, and it would
  **post a large, entirely fictional month of revenue** — money collected months ago, counted twice. Since
  TASK-066 revenue now posts at the point of sale, *anything* that creates entitlement through the sale path
  inherits that. **Import must be distinguishable from sale, by construction.**
- That is also the honest answer to the backfill question: it isn't "re-post the missing days", it's
  **"entitlement comes across, revenue does not"** — the stakeholder's own *"รายได้นับใหม่ได้บ้าง บางอย่าง"*.

**Not routing it to you as work yet** — 5 questions are open (the money rule, how much history, volume, the
go-live date, and a look at the actual spreadsheet). Building an importer before seeing the real file would be
guessing at the only part that matters. **Sizing and design are yours once the answers land.** I'd rather you
know now, while the money model is fresh, than meet this a week before launch.

  be a product code we don't recognise. Now: `1 vouchers (generic hours…); 1 sales whose reference no longer
  resolves; 1 ⚠️ unrecognised product code`. That's Sober's standing "a fourth code must never fall silently
  into unattributed" — enforced in the output, not in a comment.

  map serving both reports. Explicit in the type, pinned by a test. Also documented the intended consequence:
  **customer-spend's total is less than the month total** when a sale has no resolvable student — it has no
  customer, and stays visible in revenue's `unattributed` where the money reconciles.
- **Read-only `public` declarations** carry the ownership rule as a header comment and declare **only the
  columns the join needs** — a narrow declaration can't tempt anyone into writing through it and won't drift
  when scheduling-back adds a column. No migration here touches `public`.

  would literally **move revenue between months**. Fixed +07:00; tests on **both** sides of **both** boundaries
  plus December (month 12 → next year, not month 13). Also bounded the SQL to the month via
  `bangkokMonthRangeUtc` rather than reading every SALE movement ever — derived from the **same** offset, with
  a test that predicate and range agree at **all four boundary instants across four months**, so a row can't be
  fetched-then-dropped or missed.

difference between testing an invariant and testing that it survives the interesting inputs. And he improved on

columns — with the reason written down (*"a narrow declaration can't tempt anyone into writing through it, and

endpoints (customer-spend pairs **a child's name with what their family paid** — the most sensitive join in the
product) and a **required** `month` (a report with an implicit window is a report two people read differently).

**🔐 The neighbour he flagged is a real security gap and I cut TASK-068 for it.** `GET /bo/reports/pl` has
**no `adminAuth`** — verified: every write in `bo.ts` is guarded, all reads are open by a legacy comment. For an
item list that was defensible. **For the P&L it means REQ-014's own access-control answer — "finance is
executive-only because it lives on the backoffice" — is false at the API level.** The frontend login is not
protection; an API is not protected by the UI in front of it. He was right to flag and not touch it. I checked
the thing that would have made the fix risky: backoffice-front attaches the JWT in an **axios interceptor on
every request**, so guarding reads breaks nothing. TASK-068 also asks him to judge the other financial reads
(`movements` is money history) rather than sweep them, and to **update the header comment** — otherwise the next
person re-opens a route on its authority.

**TASK-063 → DONE.** The one trap in that task was the percentage base, and she avoided it: **percentages are of
`total`, not of `known`**. Dividing by `known` would have inflated every slice precisely when demographics are
mostly blank — i.e. on day one, when someone forms their first impression of the number. The `unknown` bucket
also survives the zero-filter, so "nobody is unknown" is *stated* rather than inferred from an absence, and the
unknown label comes from her dictionary keyed on the stable key — the mid-task correction I added after
reviewing TASK-062, applied exactly as meant.

**TASK-050 → DONE, and it closes the last LOW in the backlog.** Parent `note` is finally reachable — **that was
my TASK-049 spec error**, where I listed a field the contract never provided and Fern correctly omitted it
rather than inventing an endpoint. Student-create now takes demographics so one call is enough; the comment
records *why* (a failure between create and PATCH left a student with no demographics), which is what makes it
maintainable. It waited behind six higher-value tasks without jumping the queue, exactly as filed. Raised
**TASK-069** (LOW) so the FE can drop its extra PATCH and add the note field.

### Q2 — **does the pricing rework carry the program through to the sale? Not today. It must, and it should.**

Today the product code is `course-{size}` — **the sale records the package, not the program.** Your finding is
right and it's a second, independent way REQ-014's input is missing.

**But the fix and the pricing requirement are the same change.** The real card prices **per program × package**,
so the item that holds a price must be per program × package anyway: **`course-{program}-{size}`**
(`course-onewheel-6`, `course-balanceplay-group-6`, …). Once the code carries the program:
- each price on the card has exactly one item to live on — which is what pricing needs regardless;
- **`external_ref` itself names the sport**, so revenue-by-activity becomes a **direct read of the sale**, not a
  join through `refId` → course → bookings → subject. Strictly better than SPEC-021's design, and it fixes the
  voucher asymmetry's cousin: a course sale's sport is known **at the moment of sale** instead of inferred later.

**So yes — REQ-014 unblocks as a side effect, and you can stop worrying about it.** I'll rewrite SPEC-021's
attribution to prefer the product code and keep the `refId` join only as a fallback for pre-rework sales. The
`unattributed` bucket stays (vouchers are still generic hours).

**One thing I want to state before it's decided by accident: import must not go through the sale path.** Since
TASK-066, revenue posts at the point of sale — so REQ-025's "continue an existing course" **must create
entitlement without creating a movement**, by construction, not by remembering to skip a step. That's the same
requirement as your *"entitlement comes across, revenue does not"*, and it means **pricing and REQ-025 touch the
same code and should be designed together.** I'll spec them as one pair.

**Your VAT flag is exactly the kind of thing I'd have got wrong silently** — all figures gross, posted as-is,
any net figure derived. It'll be a named constant with that sentence on it, not a bare number.

  between identical requests. The Vouchers tab's `q` is a **post-load JS filter** — it exists but buys nothing,
  the whole table is still read.

**⚠️ The design constraint that shaped the spec, and it's the kind of thing that only shows up if you look:**
`getCourses()` has **four** consumers — the route, the attention checks (TASK-053), eligible-students
(TASK-051) and the SOM report (TASK-062). **Three of them need the full list.** Paging it naively would silently
truncate a digest count, an eligibility list and a dashboard figure — three quiet wrongnesses in features we
shipped this week. So **paging is opt-in** (no options ⇒ today's behaviour exactly), while the **stable
`ORDER BY` is unconditional**, because non-determinism helps nobody and a deterministic order is what makes
paging mean anything at all.

**One search rule across all three tabs** is the spine of the task. Three tabs with three definitions of
"search" is how "the system is broken" gets said a second time. I also carried the walk-in warning forward —
the student lookup must **LEFT JOIN parents**, or a student with `parent_id = null` (nullable **by design**)
vanishes from every search on the page. Third time that pattern has come up this week.

**Left to Fern's judgement rather than specified:** column sorting and URL-persisted filters (the REQ marks both
✅). They're presentation, and she's closer to what the page can carry before it stops being "ไม่อึดอัด".
