- **`id` as the final `ORDER BY` tiebreak.** I specified name + `createdAt`; he noticed that isn't necessarily
  unique and **a nearly-total order still lets a row appear on two pages or none** — the precise bug paging is
  meant to prevent, and the kind that surfaces months later as "the list is wrong".
- **The counts share the items' joins and filter**, so `total` can't disagree with the page. Two queries
  drifting is how a paginator starts lying.

Plus a guard worth more than the feature: a test that the **no-argument** query carries no `limit`, `offset` or
params — so if anyone later bakes paging into the default, the suite fails **before** a digest count, an
eligibility list or a dashboard figure can silently truncate.

**⚠️ His fifth-occurrence flag, and I acted on it rather than nodding.** The whole-module `mock.module` stub in
`api.teacher-routing.test.ts` leaks process-wide, so an unrelated test file decides what a new test file may
import. Five hits now (TASK-053, TASK-062, twice before, this). Four times he patched the stub; this time he
routed around it structurally. Right call for the task — **but routing around it means the next person meets it
fresh, and it has now shaped the module layout of five tasks.** Filed as **TASK-072** (narrow fake), **LOW**,
behind the entire go-live queue, because it costs time rather than correctness. I also told him that if a narrow
fake can't express what that test needs, **an honest "this needs DI in the route module first" beats a cleverer
stub** — I'd rather re-scope than watch him out-engineer a fixture a sixth time.

**Ball:** **@Fern — TASK-071** (the pair; then TASK-065, TASK-069). **@Jason — queue is empty again**: next up
is **REQ-020 Stage 2**, which I'm speccing now. **@Porter — the new pair is in the manifest**; deploy cadence
remains my one ask with 19 days to go-live.

## [evening-22] Porter (PM) — 🔴 I TOOK THE SITE DOWN. Post-mortem + the check that would have caught it → @Sober

**The frontoffice was unusable for a period this evening. It was my failure, not a build defect.** Recording it
in full because the stakeholder's response was *"ขอร้องนะ รอบคอบหน่อย"*, and "I'll be more careful" is not a
control.

### What happened
`GET /teachers` → **500**, `column "external_ref" does not exist` (42703). Every `bo.item` query dead. Because
the teacher list reads the freelance ceiling, **one missing column took out the core screen and everything
behind it.**

### Why
TASK-066 added `0005_bo_item_external_ref` in backoffice-back. **My PENDING DEPLOY manifest said
"✅ No DB migration and no new env var in this batch."** True when I wrote it — and I never updated it when
TASK-066 landed, because I was mid-conversation about a name and a price list. The stakeholder deployed trusting
my line, the new code queried a column that wasn't there.

**I broke my own written rule** — *"add a line the moment a task is DONE, not at deploy time"* — which I put on
the board specifically to stop this.

### The part I want you to take from it
**"Nothing to do here" is the most dangerous cell in any checklist**, because it's the only one people act on
*without checking*. Every other row invites verification; that one invites trust. And unlike a wrong instruction,
it produces **no symptom until the thing it was protecting fails.** Same shape as the three silent stoppages you
named today — I wrote about that pattern this morning and then produced another instance of it by lunchtime.

### And then I made it worse, live
I told her to run **`migrate:bo`**. That is the **ops→bo DATA** script; the schema migrator is **`db:migrate`**.
It failed with the same error, costing another round-trip **during an outage**. I had read both scripts earlier
today and still confused them, because I reached for the name I remembered from the board instead of opening
`package.json`. **Recalling under pressure is exactly when I should be checking.**

### Controls added — not promises
1. 🔴 **A permanent MIGRATION CHECK block at the top of the manifest.** States the rule
   (*"no migration" is a claim, not a state — it expires the moment another task lands*), gives a 10-second
   file-count-vs-journal check needing **no database access**, and puts `db:migrate` vs `migrate:bo` in a table
   so nobody repeats my mistake at 500-error o'clock.
2. ✅ **Audited both repos immediately: scheduling 15 = 15, backoffice 6 = 6.** No unregistered migrations —
   so there is no second bomb of this class waiting. I checked rather than assumed, which is the whole point.
3. **The manifest now names which repo carries a migration**, instead of a global "no migration" claim. A
   per-target fact is falsifiable; a blanket reassurance is not.

### One thing for you
The migration lives in **backoffice-back** (correct — it owns `bo`) but the code that needs it is in
**scheduling-back**. **A cross-repo schema dependency is invisible in both repos' own deploy steps.** I've
covered it here procedurally, but if there's a way to make scheduling **fail loudly at startup** when `bo` is
behind — rather than 500 on the first user request — that would be worth more than my checklist. **Your call
whether it's worth the work; I'm raising it, not requesting it.**

**Site is back up, confirmed by the stakeholder. Ball: @Sober** — REQ-013 continues per [evening-21].

everything else is behind a pager. We would have shipped the fix-the-bookings-page REQ with its headline symptom
intact.

mechanism, to say so — the goal is "staff see what's coming", and his read beats my first guess at how.

deciding it in either direction. Non-blocking; REQ-024 ships either way.

subtly wrong (invented `bo.item.externalRef`, promised retroactive attribution, devolved a stakeholder ✅), and
each time an engineer built exactly what I said. **They're following instructions faithfully; the defects are
entering at my end.** Verifying their work catches their mistakes — it doesn't catch mine. What has caught mine
every time is an engineer stopping to check the premise. That's worth protecting more than throughput.

## [21:45] Jason (BE)
- **TASK-070 → DONE** (Sober). **TASK-073 → REVIEW.** tsc 0 · **`bun test` 299/0** (was 293, **+6**).
- 🔴 **I changed the default and flagged it for Sober's ruling.** His DoD says *"no `sort` → **newest/upcoming**
  first"* as if those were one thing — **for this data they are opposites.** `courseSessionDates` books a
  session **every week for `size` weeks forward at registration**, so a 10-session course puts rows **10 weeks
  out** the moment it's sold ⇒ the *newest* booking is routinely **2–3 months away**, and `date_desc` would
  open page 1 on **November while it's August** — wrong in the opposite direction from the oldest-first bug it
  replaces, and just as useless to staff.
- His alternative (`date_asc` + default `from=today`) does show what's coming, but by **hiding the past by
  default** — that's a *filter* change, not a sort. Staff do look back (yesterday's attendance, a parent asking
  about last month), and a default that silently drops rows is the exact class of bug we keep getting burned by.
- **So I shipped a third mode and made it the default: `upcoming`** — today/future **soonest-first**, then the
  past **most-recent-first**. Page 1 opens on the next thing that happens, **nothing is hidden**, and it stays
  a **pure sort**: no filter, no rows removed, `total` unchanged in every direction. `date_asc`/`date_desc`
  still exist exactly as specced. ⚠️ **This overrides the literal DoD — reverting to a `date_desc` default is
  one word in `validation.ts`.** I'd rather be told I over-read the intent than ship page 1 pointing at November.
- **Total order in all three directions** (ends with `id`) — same reasoning as `/courses`: a *nearly* total
  order lets a row appear on two pages or on none, which gets reported as "the list is haunted", not as a bug.
- **`total` still agrees with the page:** count query untouched, plus a test asserting **no sort direction adds
  a `where`** — so a future edit can't quietly turn sorting into filtering. Invalid `sort` → clean 400.
- **Printed and read the generated SQL** rather than trusting my own assertions: binds today to `$1`/`$2`,
  valid Postgres (string↔`date` comparison is already how the existing `from`/`to` filters work).
- **TASK-072 (leaky test stub) deliberately NOT taken yet** — its own status says *"behind the entire go-live
  queue"*, and that queue still has **TASK-071 + TASK-074 open with Fern**. BE's slice being clear isn't the
  same as the queue being clear, and TASK-072 costs time, not correctness. It's next once the batch lands.
- **Waiting on @Sober:** TASK-073 (incl. the default ruling) + TASK-070 shipped-with note + TASK-050.

## Sober (SA Lead) — SPEC-023 written: REQ-020 Stage 2 (the go-live security item)
Jason's queue empties after TASK-073, so I specced the next go-live item. This is the one I'd most regret
deferring, for a reason that is about timing rather than severity: **22 of ~23 teachers are unlinked, and they
will all link during the run-up to launch.** The window in which an unverified claim is possible and the
fortnight in which every teacher walks through it are **the same fortnight**.

**The vulnerability is one line.** `line-webhook.service.ts:174` — `db.update(teachers).set({ lineUserId })` on
a nickname match. Typing a teacher's nickname grants that teacher's access, on the spot, to whoever typed it.
Stage 1 (TASK-047) stopped the two worst symptoms; **Stage 2 removes the assumption underneath: that typing a
name proves you are that person.**

**Design — every claim becomes a request, and approval is the only writer of `teachers.lineUserId`.** One
writer means "how did this account get linked?" has exactly one answer. And the part I like: **the ambiguous
case stops being a dead end and becomes the feature.** Stage 1 could only refuse a nickname collision; with a
queue, a collision is simply a request with **no teacher attached**, and staff pick who it is — which is what
Porter's original design asked for and Stage 1 couldn't deliver alone.

**Notification by reuse, not by building.** A **9th attention check** (`pending_teacher_links`) puts the queue
where staff already look every morning — no new channel, no new plumbing. That matters practically: ~22 requests
arrive in a fortnight, and **a queue nobody is told about is a queue nobody empties.** It's also the third time
REQ-023's registry has paid for itself, which is the strongest argument that the amendment I made to SPEC-018
was worth measuring rather than assuming.

**Two safety rules I wrote as requirements rather than notes**, because they're the ways this design fails
quietly: a claim on an **already-linked** teacher must be refused **at request time** (never become a pending
request that would silently steal an account on approval), and approval must **re-check** — between request and
decision the teacher may have been linked, archived or renamed, and approving must fail cleanly rather than
overwrite. I also told Jason the bot must not become an oracle: a request naming a real teacher and one naming
nobody must look **identical** to the person typing.

**One question I'm genuinely routing rather than deciding: notify the teacher on unlink?** I'm proceeding on
**no push** — unlink usually means the person has left, and messaging them *"your access was removed"* is a
real-world social act, not a technical one. Easy to add later; **hard to un-send.** That asymmetry is why it's
the one question here I want an actual answer to.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-073** (tiny) then **TASK-075**. **@Fern — TASK-074**, then TASK-076, TASK-065,
TASK-069. **@Porter — two questions** (URL-persisted filters; notify-on-unlink) and deploy cadence.
**Queue after this: pricing + REQ-025 designed as a pair** — that's the last of the go-live list.

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-075 reviewed → DONE (the go-live security item is built)
`tsc` 0 · `bun test` **316/0** · `drizzle/*.sql` **16** = `_journal.json` **16 tags**. My own runs.

**🔐 I verified the single-writer property myself rather than accept his grep**, because it is the entire point
of the task. Every write to `teachers.lineUserId` in `src/`: the **grant** at `teacher-link.service.ts:132`
(inside `approveTeacherLinkRequest`, after `decideApproval`), and otherwise **`null`** — unlink and the role
move. Nothing else. `line-webhook.service.ts:174` is gone, replaced by a comment naming what used to be there,
which is the right way to leave a removed vulnerability: the next person reading that function learns why it
looks like this instead of re-adding the convenience.

**Two judgement calls he made that I had not specified, both reasoned from principles rather than instructions:**
- **Archived teachers read as `not-found`, and archived duplicates are filtered *before* the ambiguity count.**
  A departed namesake would otherwise turn a real single match into a fake collision — and replying "that
  teacher exists but has left" **leaks roster information to an unauthenticated stranger**. He arrived at the
  oracle rule's logic in a case the spec never mentioned.
- **One PENDING per account enforced by a partial unique index** — DB-enforced rather than remembered, partial
  so historical decisions survive as an audit trail. I specified a behaviour; he made it a property.

it by spreading) with a 6-char ref instead — I asked for a queue, he asked what the screen actually *needs*.
And `candidates` per row means the FE **physically cannot** approve a collision without naming someone.

**The three additions he had to make are the shape of the change:** the roster move, the rich menu and the
approval notice all hung off "the moment of binding", and the binding moved. Sending the approval notice
*because the bot promises it* is the detail I would have missed — *"not sending would have made the bot's own
message a lie"* is the right standard, and it's the same instinct as TASK-066's loud logging.

rule as TASK-058/059 — **never ship a new gate without the screen that opens it** — now written into the

## [later-9] Fern (FE) — TASK-076 built (teacher LINE-link approval queue) → REVIEW → @Sober

Startup ritual: re-read board + today's log. **TASK-071 came back DONE** (thank you — and noted on the sort:

- **Placement (you left it to me):** its own nav page **`/scheduler/link-requests`**, directly after People,
  labelled "LINE links" / "ผูก LINE". The People screen is a **parents** list, so a teacher queue bolted on
  would have been a section about the wrong people. **I also put unlink on this same page** — granting a link
  and removing one are the same decision from either end, so "how do I stop a departed teacher getting pushes?"
  has one answer; it also gives the page a job once the launch fortnight empties the queue.
- **The collision is made impossible, not discouraged:** the candidate picker is **inline in the card** with a
  one-line reason, **Approve stays `disabled` until a teacher is named**, and candidates render as **full name +
  nickname** so staff can tell two ดิว apart. The ordinary case still approves in **one click**. I also made the
  **mock throw `teacher-required`** like your server, so a future UI bug can't look like it works offline.
- **Built against your actual routes**, read from `api.ts` + `teacher-link.service.ts` rather than inferred:
  list/approve/reject + `DELETE /teachers/:id/line-link`. Every decision invalidates queue + roster + attention,
  so the pending count can't disagree with the list staff just emptied.
- **Browser-checked the whole path** (mock, localhost verified first): read the buttons' disabled state —
  only the collision was blocked → opened the picker `[ครูดิว ธนพล | ครูดิว ณัฐวรรณ]` → picking enabled Approve →
  approved → queue 3→2 and **ครูดิว ณัฐวรรณ, the one I chose, appeared as linked** · rejected ฟ้า (2→1, **not**

## Sober (SA Lead) — SPEC-024 written: per-program pricing (and it closes REQ-014's blocker)
Last of the go-live list, and the half that had to come first. **Until this lands every course and voucher sale
posts a wrong number** — and since TASK-066, sales post from the first day of go-live. Porter's placeholders
were +16 % to +54 %, growing with package size.

**Reading the card structurally changed the design: there are four PRICE GROUPS, not eight programs.** Six
programs (Bike/Scooter · Balance Cruiser · Surfskate · Freeskate · Skateboard · Inline) share one price line;
Onewheel, Balance Play private and Balance Play group each have their own. Keying prices by *subject* would
create ~24 items where **10** are needed, and a seventh skate program would need a price **invented** rather
than inherited. So the item is keyed on the group, and `subjects.price_group` is a one-column migration —
**data, not code**, because the owner adds programs.

**⚠️ The part I'm most pleased with, and it's reuse rather than design: availability falls out of the
catalogue.** Onewheel has no 10 h and Balance Play no 4 h, so **those items simply don't exist** — and
TASK-066's `isKnownSaleItem` already refuses an unknown code **loudly**. Staff can currently sell a package the
school doesn't offer; afterwards the impossible combination has nothing to sell. **No availability table, no new
rule** — a guard Jason built for a different reason turns out to answer this one exactly.

**Porter's Q2 is answered concretely: yes, the sale will carry the program — and that unblocks REQ-014.**
Revenue-by-sport becomes a **direct read of `external_ref`** instead of a join back through refId → course →
bookings → subject. That's *better* than SPEC-021's original design: a course sale's sport becomes known **at
the moment of sale** rather than inferred afterwards. I'll rewrite SPEC-021's attribution to prefer the product
code once TASK-077 lands, keeping the refId join as a fallback for pre-rework sales.

**VAT gets a named constant with the sentence on it**, not a bare number. Porter flagged gross-vs-net loudly and
he was right to: it is exactly the assumption that gets made silently in a pricing constant and then quietly
misstates every report built on top of it.

**One dependency the owner should know about:** subjects can't be created in the UI (known limit since REQ-003),
so `price_group` is set by migration. **If she adds a program before go-live, someone must set its group or its
sales refuse.** The refusal is loud and safe — but it's a dependency, and she should hear it in one line rather
than meet it on a Tuesday.

**And the coupling I promised to design for:** since revenue posts at the point of sale, **import must be a
different verb, not the same verb with a flag.** A `skipRevenue: true` boolean is one forgotten default away
from posting a fictional launch month for money collected in Excel months ago. SPEC-025 will specify a separate
explicit endpoint — recorded in SPEC-024 too, because *this* spec is what makes the sale path charge correctly,
and that is precisely what import must not do.

**Staged honestly:** voucher exclusions (no Onewheel / no Balance Play) are **Stage 2, after go-live** — real,
but they misprice a *booking* rather than every sale. Equipment rental stays unscoped, noted so it isn't lost.

**Ball:** **@Jason — TASK-077** (go-live queue). **@Fern — TASK-074/078**, then TASK-065, TASK-069.
**@Porter — one line to the owner** about the price-group dependency, plus the two open questions and cadence.
**Next from me: SPEC-025** — the last thing on the go-live list.

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-073 + TASK-074 reviewed → DONE. REQ-024 is complete.
`tsc` 0 both repos; `bun test` **316/0**. My own runs.

**🔴 Jason found a defect in my DoD, not in his code, and overrode it correctly.** I wrote *"returns
newest/upcoming first"* as if those were one thing. **For this data they are opposites:** `courseSessionDates`
writes a booking every week for `size` weeks forward at registration, so a 10-session course puts rows **ten
weeks out** the moment it's sold, and the newest booking in the table is routinely months away.
**`date_desc` would have opened page 1 on November while it's August** — wrong in the opposite direction from
the oldest-first bug it was replacing, and just as useless. **I would have shipped that.**

**He also rejected my own suggested alternative for the better reason.** `date_asc` + a default `from = today`
does show what's coming — but it does it by **hiding the past**, which is a *filter* wearing a sort's clothes.
Staff do look backwards, and **a default that silently drops rows is precisely the class of failure this team
has been burned by three times this week**. He named that pattern without being pointed at it.

What he shipped — `upcoming`: today/future soonest-first, then past most-recent-first — opens page 1 on the next
thing that happens, hides **nothing**, and leaves `total` unchanged in every direction. The SQL expresses two
orders in one pass by letting past rows go NULL on the second key; neat, and no union.

**On overriding a literal DoD: he did it the only acceptable way** — named the line, gave the evidence, and said
how to revert in one word. I've told him to do that every time. **A DoD is my best guess at the goal, and when
the code disagrees with my guess, the code usually knows something I didn't.** That's now happened four times
today from three different directions, which stops being coincidence and starts being the operating condition.

**TASK-074:** a **clickable column header rather than a fifth control** — so Fern's crowding argument from
TASK-071 doesn't come true — server-side, resets to page 1 with every other filter, and the toggle is
**upcoming ⇄ oldest**, which is what staff actually mean. `date_desc` stays in the API but has no reason to be
one click away.

**REQ-024 is complete** (070 · 071 · 073 · 074) and ships as one indivisible batch. I put the headline check
into acceptance in the customer's own terms: **page 1 must start at the next session, not the oldest booking in
the system** — then flip the header and confirm the count **doesn't change**, because that is what proves it's a
sort and not a filter.

**Go-live status: REQ-013 ✅ built · REQ-024 ✅ built · REQ-020 Stage 2 ✅ built · pricing specced (TASK-077/078
open) · REQ-025 next from me.** Five of five are now scoped and three are code-complete — **and still nothing is
deployed.** Two indivisible pairs in the batch now.

families as revenue faults, for a week.** That is worse than noise: it's the detector that guards real revenue,
and **a detector that cries wolf gets muted.** We would spend the launch fortnight ignoring the one check that
would tell us the sale path had broken again. Hence `source` on courses/vouchers and an explicit exclusion —
one column, which also answers "why does this course have no revenue?" for whoever asks in an audit.

the start date, and an imported course started months ago — computing would silently change what the family
bought); and **off-card sizes are importable on purpose** — the family already bought it, whatever today's card
says, so TASK-078's sellable restriction must not be reused on that form.

## [evening-28] Porter (PM) — 🔴 TWO LIVE DEFECTS from the customer presentation + REQ-007 thresholds CONFIRMED UNCHANGED → @Sober

The owner presented the system to the customer today (`smart-scheduler-requirement/20260801-meeting.md`) and
came back with defects. **Two are live, and one of them failed in front of the customer.**

### 🔴 A. The freelance budget colour strip did not appear during the demo

Her words: *"พบว่าระบบสีไม่ทำงาน ขณะโชว์ลูกค้า"* — **REQ-007, which we closed as DELIVERED.**

**Diagnosis (read-only, `FreelanceBudgetStrip.tsx:29-30` + `lib/scheduler/teacher.ts`):**
```
const tone = budgetTone(teacher.remainingMinor, teacher.budgetMinor);
if (!tone) return null;                    // no budget data → NO STRIP AT ALL
```
`budgetTone` returns `null` when `budgetMinor` is null or `<= 0`. **So a freelance teacher with no budget set
renders exactly like a teacher who is perfectly healthy: nothing.**

**I don't think the code is wrong — I think the data was missing.** Almost certainly the demo teachers had no
ceiling set (a known open item: *"real freelance budgets never verified in the FE"*). ⚠️ **Also worth checking
against TASK-060**: closing a ceiling on type-change/archive sets `active=false`, so `findFreelanceItem` returns
nothing → `setupIncomplete` → **no strip.** If anyone's type was changed or they were archived/reactivated,
their strip vanished by design. **Please confirm which it was rather than assuming the benign one.**

**But the real defect is the design, not the data:**
> **"No budget configured" and "budget is fine" render identically — as nothing.**

That is **the same failure class as everything else today**: unregistered jobs, `void recordSale`, the swallowed
`400`. A freelance teacher whose ceiling was never set is exactly the case an admin most needs to see, and it is
the one case the UI is silent about. It should be **visibly unconfigured**, not absent. The customer-facing cost
is already proven: it read as *"the feature doesn't work"* in front of the customer, and I can't argue with that
reading.

### 🔴 B. LINE check-in returns "ไม่มีคาบให้เช็คอิน" after she created and confirmed a booking

`findTodayBookingsForParent` filters **`date = today` AND `status = CONFIRMED` AND `studentId ∈ this parent's
children`**. Empty ⇒ `empty_checkin`. So one of three things is true, and **the message cannot tell them apart**:
1. the booking isn't for **today** (her earlier screenshot showed bookings dated **2026-07-01**);
2. it isn't **CONFIRMED**;
3. **her LINE account isn't the parent of that student** — she may be linked as a teacher, or the booking is for
   a child under a different parent record.

**The defect I'm confident of regardless of which:** *"ไม่มีคาบให้เช็คอิน"* **collapses three different
situations, plus a fourth** — a booking today that is CONFIRMED but outside the check-in window
(`CHECKIN_EARLY_MINUTES = 30`, so it opens only 30 min before start and closes at end). A parent who arrives
45 minutes early is told they have no class. **Same silence pattern, on the flow a customer touches most.**

❓ **And a business question I'm putting to her, not deciding: is a 30-minute window right for a real counter?**

### ✅ REQ-007 thresholds: KEEP OURS. Do not change them.

The meeting doc lists <40 green / 40–79 yellow / **80–90 orange** / 100 red. **That is not a new requirement.**
Her words: *"ในเอกสารนั่นแค่สรุปจากประชุม ซึ่งเป็นการพูดแบบสมมุติ เพราะพบว่าระบบสีไม่ทำงานขณะโชว์ลูกค้า"* —
the numbers were spoken as an example **because the strip wasn't rendering to point at.** The delivered

## [later-12] Fern (FE) — TASK-081 fixed (REQ-024's last defect) → REVIEW · 🔴 and I can measure layout after all → @Sober

Startup ritual: re-read board + today's log. TASK-078 came back **DONE**; taking your order
**081 → 082 → 080 → 065 → 069**, so this is TASK-081.

### 🔴 The finding matters more than the fix: your premise was wrong, and so was mine
You wrote that our FE verification *"can prove behaviour and cannot prove appearance"*, and I'd been working on
the same assumption. **The pane doesn't paint — but the browser still computes layout.**
`getBoundingClientRect()` returns real widths and `resize_window` really reflows. **Geometry is measurable
here.** Only *painted* things — colour, contrast, overlap, font rendering — are genuinely out of reach.

**So I reproduced your defect by number rather than by picture.** With the fix in, I stripped the new `min-w`
off the two date inputs in the live DOM and re-measured the same elements:

- **with the min:** From **176 px**, To **176 px**
- **without it (what shipped):** From **36 px**, To **26 px**

26–36 px *is* the stakeholder's screenshot. **This class of defect was catchable all along and I wasn't
catching it** — that's the honest version. From now on I measure the row on any change that adds a control to
one, which is the check you asked me to write a reasoned statement about instead.

### The fix
A **min** width on every control in the row, not a bigger max. A flex item's default `min-width: auto` doesn't
protect a Mantine input, and an **empty date input has no text to hold it open** — which is precisely why the
Selects looked fine, only the pickers collapsed, and it only reproduced on CUSTOM. With a min on every item a
full row **wraps instead of crushing**, so it can't happen to whatever gets added next either. Comment left at
the row recording the invariant and the numbers.

Measured: **1600** all 7 controls on one line (From/To 176 each) · **1280** presets one line, CUSTOM wraps
From/To onto their own line at 176 · **768** wraps, min control 160 · **375** one per line, min 160, **no
horizontal overflow**. Filtering re-verified unchanged (From = 2 ส.ค. → "พบ 2 รายการ"); layout-only change.

⚠️ **One deviation from your DoD, said out loud:** the presets hold one line at **≥1280**, but **below ~1270
they now wrap** where before they shrank. Deliberate — shrinking *is* the defect — and I tightened the mins to
push that threshold as low as I could. Five usable controls need ~800px however you arrange them. **Holding one
line down to 1024 needs the filters restructured, not retuned; I did not do that, and it's your scope call.**
I don't think the row is doing too much: it fits seven controls on a wide monitor and degrades by wrapping.

**@Sober — TASK-081 is REVIEW. REQ-024 closes on it.** Moving on to **TASK-082** now unless you say otherwise.

One note on [evening-28] **defect A** (freelance strip): that's FE and it'll be mine — I've **not** touched it,
it hasn't been cut. When it is, note I can now **measure** whether a strip renders and at what size, so
"configured-and-fine" vs "never configured" being indistinguishable is testable, not just arguable.

## Sober (SA Lead) — TASK-081 DONE, and Fern disproved a structural claim I made this morning
`tsc` 0 · `bun run build` success (my runs); `min-w` on every control in the filter row.

**🔴 The correction is worth more than the fix, and it's mine to own.** I told the team our FE verification
*"can prove behaviour and cannot prove appearance"* and instructed Fern to compensate with a reasoned statement
about narrow widths. **She showed the premise is wrong.** The pane doesn't *paint*, but the browser still
**computes layout** — `getBoundingClientRect()` returns real widths, `resize_window` genuinely reflows.

**And she didn't merely assert it: she reproduced this morning's shipped defect by measurement**, stripping her
`min-w` off in the live DOM and re-measuring — **176 px → 36 px and 26 px**. That *is* the stakeholder's
screenshot, arrived at from a number instead of a picture. **A class of defect that reached production today is
catchable here from now on.**

**I've made it a standing rule at the top of the board**, not a note in one task: any FE change that adds or
resizes a control in a shared row **measures the row at 1600 / 1280 / 768 / 375 and reports the numbers**. And I
recorded that it replaces what I told them, because a wrong instruction from me will otherwise outlive the
correction.

Her caveat stands and I kept it verbatim: **measurement catches geometry, not paint** — colour, contrast,
overlap remain invisible, so **a deployed look is still the only full detector.** She narrowed the gap rather
than closing it, and said so.

**The fix itself is structural rather than local:** *a min on every item, not a bigger max.* With a minimum on
every flex item the row **wraps** when it runs out of space and can no longer crush anything, whatever gets
added next — which was my "the next control will hit this again" concern, answered properly. The comment
carrying the measurements at the row is what stops someone re-breaking it while tidying classes.

**Her deliberate DoD deviation — accepted:** below ~1270 px the presets now wrap to a second line where they
previously stayed on one by shrinking. **Wrapping is the correct failure mode; shrinking is the defect.**
Holding one line by crushing controls would be re-introducing the bug in a milder form. Answered her question
too: **no restructuring now** — the row degrades correctly, and with 19 days left I'm not rebuilding a working
filter bar to save a wrap at 1200 px.

**That's twice today an engineer has upgraded the process by disproving something I stated confidently** —
Jason on the "newest first" DoD, Fern on layout verifiability. Both times I was confident and wrong in a way
that would have shaped how the team worked for months. **The thing protecting us isn't my review; it's that
they argue back with evidence.**

**REQ-024 has nothing open.** Ball: **@Fern — TASK-082 → TASK-080 → TASK-065 → TASK-069.**
**@Porter — the corrected A–G list stands**, and TASK-081 closes item A.

## [evening-29] Porter (PM) — 🔴 CHECK-IN BUG PINNED: an expired token is never reissued → the booking can NEVER be checked in → @Sober

**Updating [evening-28] B — she retried and got a different message: "โทเคนเช็คอินหมดอายุแล้ว".** That changes
the diagnosis from "which of three silent causes" to a **specific, reproducible defect. Not a data problem.**

**`services/checkin.service.ts` — `getCheckinQr()`:**
```ts
if (!row.checkinToken) {
  const issued = await issueCheckinToken(bookingId);   // only when NONE exists
  return formatCheckinPayload(row, issued.token, issued.expiresAt);
}
return formatCheckinPayload(row, row.checkinToken, ...);  // ← returns it even if EXPIRED
```
Tokens expire at **end of class** (`tokenExpiryIso(row.date, row.endTime)`), which is right. But the reissue
branch is guarded on **existence, not validity**. So once a booking has ever had a token and that time passes,
`getCheckinQr` hands back a **dead token**, and `checkinByToken` correctly rejects it —
`"โทเคนเช็คอินหมดอายุแล้ว"`. **Permanently. That booking can never be checked in through LINE again.**

**Why it survived to a customer demo — this is the part I'd want the team to sit with:**
> **Testing with a fresh booking can never reproduce it.** A new booking has no token, so the code takes the
> issue branch and works perfectly. The bug needs a booking that was *touched once and then went stale* — which
> is a rare state in testing and **the normal state in production.**

Her live case is exactly that shape: a booking from **2026-07-01** whose token died a month ago.

⚠️ **And check the neighbours before calling it fixed** — the same "guard on existence, not validity" shape may
sit on the **QR check-in page** and anywhere else calling `getCheckinQr`. I'm not going to guess how many; I'd
rather ask than have this come back on 20 August. **Fix is yours to design** — I'll only say that "reissue when
absent **or** expired" is the behaviour a parent expects, and that the expiry itself is correct and shouldn't be
loosened to dodge the bug.

**Scope note:** this makes check-in the **third** thing today whose failure mode was invisible until someone
used it for real. It is also the flow **a paying customer touches most often**, and it failed during a
presentation. **I'd put it at the top of the queue.** Priority is mine to call; sequencing remains yours.

**Ball: @Sober.**

## [evening-30] Porter (PM) — 🔴 LINE "แจ้งลา" dies silently — the ONE handler with no try/catch · + REQ-029 · + check-in window ±30 → @Sober

Three things. The first is a live defect found by the owner minutes ago.

### 🔴 A. "กดแจ้งลา แล้วหายไปเลย ไม่ขยับอะไรเลย" — no reply at all, and I think I know why

`line-webhook.service.ts` — compare the two sibling handlers:

```ts
// doCheckinBooking  ✅ guarded
try { const result = await checkinByToken(qr.token); … }
catch (e: any) { return send(replyToken, [textReply(e?.message ?? t("checkin_err", lang), lang)]); }

// doLeaveBooking  ❌ NOT guarded
const result = await updateBookingStatus(b.id, "sick-leave", "แจ้งลาผ่าน LINE");   // ← can throw
return send(replyToken, [textReply(t("leave_ok", …), lang)]);
```

**`updateBookingStatus` throws on the business rules we deliberately built** — the advance leave-notice rule
(`lib/leave-notice.ts`: FT/PT ≥1h, FL ≥2h before start) and the over-quota lock. When it throws, **the reply is
never sent**, so LINE shows *nothing at all*: exactly *"หายไปเลย ไม่ขยับอะไรเลย"*.

**The likely trigger in her test:** leave requested inside the notice window. **The rule fired correctly — and
the parent was told nothing.** A rule that works and says nothing is indistinguishable from a broken button,
which is the same conclusion we reached on REQ-019's sale modals **twice**. This is the fourth instance today.

⚠️ **Please check the other branches while you're in there** rather than fixing only this one — check-in is
guarded, leave is not, and I don't know about `children` / `register` / the teacher-side handlers. **A per-handler
guard is exactly the kind of thing that gets added where someone remembered.** Whether the right answer is
one boundary at the webhook entry point instead of four `try`s is yours to judge.

**Secondary finding, separate but real:** `doLeave` calls `findTodayBookingsForParent` — **leave only works for
TODAY's bookings.** Yet the product has always promised *"การแจ้งลาล่วงหน้า"*, the whole notice rule is about
warning us *before* the day, and the owner has just asked to record **known future absences at purchase time**.
**A parent cannot report tomorrow's absence through LINE at all.** I'm not scoping the fix; I want it named
before someone closes A and calls leave "working".

### 🆕 B. REQ-029 — voucher booking silently records the wrong sport (+ student search)

Verified in `BookingModal.tsx:582-623`: a VOUCHER booking takes `subjectOptions[0]` — **the teacher's first
subject** — as the program. A coach who teaches Surfskate, Skateboard and Inline produces bookings that all
claim Surfskate.

**Why this is more than a missing dropdown:** it is **written to the booking**, it silently **corrupts
REQ-013's sport-share and REQ-014's revenue-by-activity at source**, and it makes **REQ-027's voucher
exclusions unenforceable** — you cannot forbid a voucher on Onewheel while the program is auto-filled.
Course is correct and should stay (it takes the subject from the course itself). Also adds the student search
the owner asked for on the Course/Voucher tabs. ⚠️ **Historical voucher bookings already carry a guessed
sport** — not proposing to correct them, but I'll make sure the owner knows before anyone reads those charts.

### ✅ C. Check-in window: the owner has decided — **±30 minutes**

*"เอาบวกลบ30นาที"* → open at **start − 30** (unchanged) and close at **end + 30** (currently closes at `end`).
`CHECKIN_EARLY_MINUTES` exists; the late side is new. Small, and it should go out with A.

## [evening-31] Porter (PM) — 🆕 REQ-030: a course is an editable PLAN, not a fixed recurrence. The biggest ask of the day → @Sober

The owner described what she actually needs from course booking, and it reframes the feature rather than
extending it. **Full REQ written: `requirements/REQ-030-course-plan-editable-recurring-schedule.md`.** Her words:

> *"ซื้อคอร์สวันเสาร์ 6 สัปดาห์ … ต้องมีตารางให้ลงเลย ว่าเสาร์นี้ครู A เสาร์หน้าขอครู B เสาร์ต่อไปกลับมาครู A
> เสาร์ต่อไปลารอไว้เลย มีนัดด่วน ต้องบวกไปสัปดาห์ต่อๆ ไป จาก 6 สัปดาห์เป็นจบสัปดาห์ที่ 7 แล้วพอกดแทรกเป็น
> วันอาทิตย์ คอร์สก็จะกลับมาจบในสัปดาห์ที่ 6"* · *"มันต้องทำงานกับ recurring date เสมอ"*

**The one sentence that matters: we model a course as a *recurrence*; the business treats it as a *plan*.**
Teacher varies by week, absences are known in advance, sessions get inserted — and **the end date is a
consequence of all of it**, not a fixed property. Today `POST /courses` generates a rigid chain and every one of
those realities has to be fought after the fact, one calendar cell at a time.

**She also answered her own earlier question** *"จองคอร์สมีไปทำไม"* — the Course tab is the mechanism for
adjusting a plan, and it is what **REQ-025's migration will use** to place a mid-course child's remaining
sessions. **REQ-025 and REQ-030 are the same machinery. Design them together.**

**She wants it in two places** — the calendar (where clashes are visible) **and** the course screen (a list of
one child's plan, which she expects to be easier for an admin). ⚠️ **Two entry points, one implementation** —
two code paths for one rule is how one policy ends up with two answers. Jason avoided exactly that on TASK-058
with a single `SUSPENDED_MESSAGE`.

### Four interactions I've asked you to design deliberately rather than meet by accident

1. 💰 **Per-session teacher × the freelance ceiling.** Changing session 2 from coach A to coach B must **release
   A and draw B in one transaction**. `reconcileFreelanceDraw` should cope — but **it was written for status
   changes, not teacher changes.** This is money; verify, don't assume.
2. ⚠️ **`MAX_WEEK_BY_SIZE` becomes load-bearing.** Planned absences push straight against the extension cap —
   and **the 6-session value ("week 8") is a documented, never-confirmed assumption** (root `CLAUDE.md`).
   A guess is about to start affecting real customers. **I'm getting it confirmed.**
3. Every edit is a **re-validation** — the DB index protects us, but the user deserves the reason before the
   constraint fires.
4. Attended history stays immutable.

### 4 questions for the owner — I'm taking them, but #1 gates the design

🔴 **Does a planned absence consume the sick-leave quota?** A 6-session course allows 2. If a family marks 3
known absences at purchase — refuse, allow freely, or lock? *(My lean: allow. The quota exists to stop
open-ended rescheduling **after** a course starts; a family telling us their dates on day one is the opposite
behaviour. But that's a commercial policy, not a technical call.)* Also: how far may a course be pushed out ·
can a **confirmed and already-notified** teacher be swapped (my lean: yes, but **notify both** — the old one
that it's off their schedule, or someone doesn't turn up) · does an inserted make-up cost the family anything.

**Don't wait on me for the others** — the shape is clear enough to start, and today's live defects (the silent
leave failure, REQ-029's guessed sport) come first regardless.

**Ball: @Sober.**

## [evening-32] Porter (PM) — REQ-030 fully answered · 🔴 NEW confirmed defect: two courses = two identical rows → @Sober

### ✅ All four REQ-030 questions answered — written into the REQ

1. 🔴 **Planned absences: COUNTED but never LOCKING. Two rules, not one — please read the REQ text.**
   *"ควรสิ"* + *"ข ตามที่นายคิดเลย"* ⇒ a planned absence **consumes the quota and earns the extension**, exactly
   like a sick day — **but exceeding the quota this way does not lock the course.** The quota keeps driving how
   the plan extends and stops being a penalty when the family declared the dates up front. The lock survives for
   what it was for: **open-ended rescheduling after a course has started.** *(I've stated my reading explicitly
   in the REQ so she can correct it rather than have us assume it.)*
2. ✅ **The 6-session extension ceiling of "week 8" is CONFIRMED CORRECT.** That closes an assumption flagged in
   the root `CLAUDE.md` since the beginning of the project. **It is now a rule, not a guess** — and planned
   absences push straight into it, so please test against it.
3. ✅ **A confirmed teacher can be swapped, both must be notified — plus a NEW rule: a minimum notice period,
   in DAYS.** *"ควรมีเวลาว่าเปลี่ยนได้ล่วงหน้ากี่วันด้วย"*. ❓ The number isn't decided; I'm getting it. Build it
   as a **named configurable rule** alongside `lib/leave-notice.ts` — note her unit is **days**, that one is hours.
4. ✅ An inserted make-up costs the family nothing, **but still draws the freelance teacher's budget.**

### 🔴 NEW — confirmed defect: a student with two courses is impossible to tell apart

Owner: *"น้อง H มีคอร์ส skate และคอร์ส freeskate … จะมีให้เลือกมั้ยว่าจะใช้โควตาคอร์สไหน"* — **added to REQ-029.**

**The backend is right and the label is wrong.** `BookingModal.tsx:740`:
```ts
data={eligible.map((e) => ({ value: entKey(e), label: e.nickname || e.name }))}
```
One row per entitlement is returned — **so the choice exists** — but the label is **only the nickname**, so two
courses render as **two rows both reading "น้อง H"**, identical on screen and identical in the searchable box.
Staff pick one at random and **the session silently draws from the wrong course's quota**, which surfaces later
as one course ending early and another that never finishes.

**Everything needed is already fetched** — the `ContextCard` under the picker shows subject · used/size ·
leaveUsed/quota · expiry, **but only after you've chosen.** The information arrives one step too late to inform
the decision. This is a labelling fix, not a data fix.

### 📌 The pattern I want to name, because it changes what should be built

**The owner has now described the same missing thing three times in different words:** "manage plan on the
booking page" · "manage plan for course *and voucher*, for this child" · "which course's quota does this insert
use". Add REQ-029's identical rows and REQ-025's migration, and it is one gap:

> **Nowhere in the product can you look at ONE CHILD'S ENTITLEMENT as a thing** — what they hold, what's left,
> which sessions belong to it, and how to move them.

REQ-030 requirement 8 now asks for exactly that, **for vouchers too** — a voucher has no recurrence, but the
admin's question is identical: *"what does this child have, and how do I move it?"* **I'd rather you designed
that one surface than four features that each work around its absence.** Your call; I'm naming the pattern, not
the solution.

**Ball: @Sober.** Priority is unchanged — today's live defects (silent leave failure, guessed voucher sport,
these identical rows) come before the plan feature.

## [evening-33] Porter (PM) — teacher-change notice = **3 days**, and she wants it configurable → 🆕 REQ-031 → @Sober

> *"3 วัน ขอให้เป็น config database แบบแก้ง่ายๆ ได้มั้ย"*

**REQ-030's open number is answered: 3 days.** And the second half is a good instinct that points wider than
the one rule, so I've written **REQ-031**.

### 🔴 The line I want to survive into the design

> **"In the database" is not the same as "easy to change".**

A value in `app_settings` with **no screen** is a constant with extra steps and a new failure mode — and
changing it would mean **running SQL against production**, which is precisely what this project forbids and what
the owner should never have to do. **A settings row without a settings screen does not satisfy her request.**
If only one half fits before go-live, build **the screen for the rules that ship**, not the mechanism for rules
that don't.

### What's already true (verified, not assumed)

- ✅ **`app_settings` exists** (`db/schema.ts:399`) and is already used for `line_admin_user_ids` — a home and a
  precedent. Not a new concept.
- The rules are **pure constants in `lib/`** consumed by **pure functions** (`leaveQuota`,
  `isWithinCheckinWindow`, `budgetTone`). Good starting shape: **keep the functions pure, pass the values in.**
  Nothing in `lib/` should learn about the database.
- ⚠️ `MAX_WEEK_BY_SIZE` is read by **`lib/recurring.ts:33`** as well as leave logic — load-bearing in two
  places, and the owner **just confirmed** its 6→week 8 value. **Confirmed values are the ones worth making
  editable; unconfirmed guesses should be settled first, not made adjustable.**

### The scope trap, named on purpose

There are **eight** hardcoded business rules (check-in window · leave quota · extension ceiling · leave notice ·
budget colours · digest time · expiring-soon thresholds · the new teacher-change notice). **Do not make all
eight configurable by reflex.** My lean: ship the mechanism + the teacher-change rule + the **check-in window**
(she has already changed that one once today, which is the only real evidence of demand we have), and move the
rest as they actually come up. **A setting nobody has ever wanted to change is a constant with extra steps.**

**Defaults stay in code; the database holds overrides.** A missing or malformed setting must fall back to the
default and say so — never to zero, null, or "no rule". That is the failure mode this project keeps paying for.

**One honest note in the REQ:** "who may change these" **cannot be enforced per-person today** — one shared
backoffice credential, and the frontoffice login doesn't distinguish users either. Same conclusion as the
meeting doc's "board-only top-up approval": **separate logins are the prerequisite**, not a rider on this REQ.

**Ball: @Sober.**

anything.

API's string appended as detail, so the screen still makes sense if that string is missing or wrong. The two
empty states are worded **differently** ("no sales this month" vs "no customer matched") so a filter miss can
never read as an empty month.

out of real environments, so a throwaway stub in her scratchpad — not in the repo, dummy token, nothing real
contacted — is the honest way to exercise a contract, and she said plainly that no live round-trip happened.

   again — **the API supplies identity, the FE supplies language** — and a composed sentence fuses the two.

   waiting to happen. The API should return reason **codes + counts + amounts**. Small, not blocking, and I

go-live list is unbuilt, and nothing left is blocking.

**So the status hasn't changed shape, only shrunk: the build is done.** What remains is deploy + acceptance
cycles and the owner's answers — including the one that must be settled **before** the batch, because if

- **TASK-079 → DONE** (Sober). **TASK-083 → REVIEW.** tsc 0 · **`bun test` 93/0** (was 87, **+6**),
  backoffice-back only.
- **Sober is right, and I should have caught it myself.** I wrote that English `unattributedReason` sentence
  in TASK-064 as an improvement — so "unattributed ฿9,000" couldn't read as "vouchers, fine" when it might be
  a product code we don't recognise. The instinct was right; **the format was the same mistake I'd already
  been shown twice** — TASK-053's missing `titleKey` and TASK-063's label key, both of which Sober fixed by
  separating identity from language. I fused them again and didn't notice. Fern flagging it rather than
  parsing it was the correct call — recovering numbers by splitting a sentence is a bug waiting to happen.
- **Built:** `unattributed: { totalMinor, reasons: [{code, count, amountMinor}] }`, and **the codes are the
  vocabulary end to end** — `AttributedSale.reason` now carries `VOUCHER`/`UNRESOLVED_REF`/`UNKNOWN_CODE`
  itself rather than an internal set translated at the edge, because two vocabularies for one concept is
  exactly how they drift. `amountMinor` per reason included: "2 sales whose ref no longer resolves" doesn't
  say whether to care; "…, ฿4,000" does.
- **Answered Sober's Question: keep the sentence for now, delete it after Fern's follow-up.** Not because
  it's worth keeping — because TASK-065 is being built against it *right now*, and removing a field mid-build
  breaks her screen for no gain. Both mirrors are `@deprecated` **and derived** from the codes, so the prose
  is a view rather than a second computation. **Recommended that Fern's follow-up render purely from codes so
  I can delete them** — I'd rather not leave a deprecated English string on a Thai screen indefinitely.
- **The second identity is accumulated, not re-derived** — `count`/`amountMinor` are tallied in the same
  single pass as `unattributedMinor`, so `sum(reasons.amountMinor) === unattributed.totalMinor` holds by
  construction. A second number is a second chance to disagree; the fix is to not compute it twice.
- Tests: `UNRESOLVED_REF` deliberately covers **two** sales (dead course ref + dead voucher ref) so count and
  sum are both exercised · 🔴 `UNKNOWN_CODE` never folded into `VOUCHER` · **both** identities incl. the two
  degenerate cases (nothing unattributable; no sales at all — `reasons` must be **empty**, not a zero row).
- **Response shape is additive** — no migration, nothing breaks before Fern's follow-up.
- **BE queue empty.** TASK-083 + TASK-050 with @Sober · TASK-072 still parked behind the go-live queue.

**He made the codes the vocabulary end to end rather than a translation at the boundary.** Mapping an internal
set to public codes at the edge would have passed this task — **two vocabularies for one concept is how they
drift**, and he closed that instead of satisfying it.

**And his answer to my "a second number is a second chance to disagree" is better than the guard I was going to
ask for:** he accumulates `count`/`amountMinor` in the **same single pass** that computes the total, so the sum
identity holds **by construction**. *"The way to stop that is to not compute it twice."*

Small things done thoughtfully: the **stable order** puts `VOUCHER` (expected) before the two faults, so a
reader's eye lands on "this is fine" before "this is wrong"; and his tests use **two** `UNRESOLVED_REF` sales so
count *and* sum are exercised rather than one row satisfying both, plus the degenerate case where `reasons` must
be **empty rather than a zero row** — which would otherwise render "0 vouchers" on a clean month.

**On his opening — he wrote that this was "the same mistake I'd already been shown twice" and didn't notice.**
Worth saying plainly, and I did: **the instinct that produced it was right.** "unattributed: ฿9,000" really does
read as "vouchers, fine" when it might be an unrecognised code, and he fixed that. The prose was the wrong
container for a right idea — **and it took a third person's screen to make the container visible.** That isn't a
lapse in attention; it's why the chain exists. What matters is that all three times the fix **generalised**
rather than being patched locally.

**Answered his question: delete both mirrors.** TASK-065 is DONE so nothing is building against them, and his
reasoning is right — *a deprecated English string on a Thai screen is how it becomes permanent.* Cut **TASK-084**
for Fern to render from codes with her own Thai wording, and told her to say when it lands so he can do the
three-line removal. I added one thing she couldn't know to ask for: **the expected case must be visually
distinguishable from the two faults**, because `VOUCHER` is normal and the other two mean someone should look.

on the go-live list.
